- **Possessing a released batch grants nothing** — no capability, entitlement, ownership, provenance, or authenticity.
- **Event payloads carry identifiers and safe metadata only** — never credentials, secrets, payment tokens, bank details, document bytes, knowledge body text, privileged narratives, cross-client content, or another Firm's data. `AggregateRoot` cannot enforce that; it is the recording event's and the owning module's obligation.
- **Satisfying this contract makes no event safe to log, dump, serialize, expose, or publish**, and **the absence of stringification and serialization members is not a confidentiality control**: `var_dump()`, reflection, stack traces, and careless logging still expose a buffered event's full contents.
- **Internal domain events are never the permanent public contract** (Constitution Article 31); the versioned `IntegrationEventEnvelope` remains `Integrations`' own.
- **Delivery is at-least-once with no exactly-once and no global-ordering claim** (Articles 34 and 43), and PF-040 delivers nothing at all.
- **AI holds no authority here** — nothing in this base grants an AI actor the ability to record, release, or suppress an aggregate's events.
- No existing security control or required check is weakened, renamed, bypassed, or removed.

**Documentation impact.** `app/Foundation/README.md`: the namespace table records `App\Foundation\Domain\Model` as **Implemented**; the accompanying note records `Model` as containing exactly `ValueObject`, `Entity`, and `AggregateRoot`; the intro sentence records PF-040 as the eighth story and the approved-order line records PF-045 as next; the `Entities` subsection records that event recording and release belong to `AggregateRoot` while **aggregate versioning belongs to neither and to no Foundation story**; the `External dependencies` section records that PF-040 introduced none and declined every dispatcher; and a new **`Aggregate roots`** subsection records the contract, the two-member rationale, the protected-recording API-integrity boundary (never an authorization or security boundary), the append-without-inspection rule, the single combined release and the named absence of every alternative, the fresh-batch and caller-copy properties, the ordering non-guarantees, the identity separation, D1's no-version decision, the no-constructor decision, the mandatory `@extends`, the full no-dispatch exclusion list, the reconstitution warning, the security position, and the test-double and concrete-aggregate prohibitions. `docs/implementation/03_Engineering_Backlog.md` and `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` updated for status and sequencing only.

**Definition of Ready (met, approved by the repository owner).** Goal clear and narrowly bounded to one base class; owner identified (repository owner); **dependencies resolved and correctly classified** — `Entity` is the parent, `DomainEvent` the recorded type and only behavioural import, `BusinessIdentifier` the `@template` bound, `Clock`/`UuidV7`/`UuidV7Generator` neither direct nor transitive code dependencies, PF-049 documentation only, `ValueObject` not a dependency at all, PF-090 through PF-093 future dependents and never dependencies, and no new dependency of any kind; **exact contract approved and recorded above**; **D1 settled** — no aggregate version, versioning being a persistence concern outside Foundation, with `Entity`'s stale docblock corrected as the sole authorized existing-source change and that change docblock-only; **D2 settled** — `recordThat()` protected and final, recording being the aggregate's own account of what it did; **D3 settled** — one combined `releaseEvents()`, with peek, read, count, clear, flush, discard, and any public recording method all prohibited rather than deferred; append-without-inspection settled (no validation, filtering, sorting, deduplication, merging, or capping); release semantics settled (recording order, atomic clear, empty repeat release, fresh subsequent batch, caller-owned returned array); ordering non-guarantees settled and recorded as non-guarantees; identity separation settled; no-constructor decision settled; generics settled on `Entity`'s already-verified invariant-template rules; exception position settled (throws nothing, adds no exception type, returns no `Result`, invents no validation); security implications identified; tests specified and deliberately proportionate; allowed and forbidden files enumerated; and **no architecture blocker** — `App\Foundation\Domain\Model` is the namespace `app/Foundation/README.md` already reserves for PF-040, the PF-049 guard is a denylist that discovers the new file automatically and permits it unchanged, and PF-090 through PF-093 already own dispatch, outbox, publication, and consumption as separate approved Sprint 0.4 stories. **The `EntityTest` directory-inventory conflict identified during implementation was resolved by explicit repository-owner authorization: the expected inventory was updated for `AggregateRoot`, with no test removed or weakened.**

**Definition of Done (met).** Acceptance criteria met. Validated in the canonical Docker PHP 8.4 application container: `composer validate --strict` valid; `composer pint:test` passed (52 files); `composer phpstan` (level 5, no errors, 36 files, **no baseline, suppression, or ignored error**, and a repository-wide scan finding zero `@phpstan-ignore` tags); an informational level-6 dry run recorded no errors, with no configuration change; the focused PF-040 suite passed (33 tests, 106 assertions); `EntityTest` passed after the owner-authorized inventory update (42 tests, 113 assertions); the PF-049 architecture guard passed **byte-identical and unchanged** (8 tests, 270 assertions); **the full test suite passed (414 tests, 1078 assertions**, up from the 381/955 baseline before this story**)**; `composer audit --locked --abandoned=report` and `npm audit --audit-level=high` both clean; `git diff --check` clean. Security and architecture were independently reviewed against this entry's requirements with no code-level P0 or P1 finding. **Independent review and correction confirmation completed; correction commit `7fbb928` passed all four required `Protect main` checks; the required human approval comment was recorded; PR #31 merged to `main` as `5ea91dc`; and the implementation branch was deleted locally and remotely.**

**Explicitly not implemented by PF-040.** **No concrete aggregate of any kind** — no `Firm`, `Matter`, `Client`, `Invoice`, `Document`, `Task`, or any other, and no name invented for one. **No concrete domain event**, in production code or otherwise; the reference event is a fixture inside the test file. PF-045 `Money` and PF-046 `Result` remain unimplemented, and no stub, placeholder, or empty directory exists for either. **No aggregate version, optimistic concurrency, expected-version check, sequence number, or concurrency token**, per D1. **No separate peek, read, count, clear, flush, discard, or public recording method**, per D3. **No event dispatch of any kind** — no dispatcher, listener, subscriber, handler, bus, queue, job, broadcaster, `ShouldQueue`, or PSR event dispatcher. No transactional outbox, publisher, consumer, dead-letter, retry, backoff, or deduplication store. No `IntegrationEventEnvelope`, delivery attempt, delivery identifier, webhook, signature, or external contract version. No persistence, repository, reconstitution, snapshotting, event sourcing, ORM mapping, Eloquent model, migration, cast, DTO, route-model binding, serialization, or wire format. No auditing, activity history, logging, telemetry, metrics, or reporting. No timestamps, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `deletedAt`, or soft deletion. No tenancy, `FirmId`, `FirmContext`, authorization, roles, permissions, ownership, or Ethical Wall decision. No `__toString`, `\Stringable`, serialization, hashing, or ordering member. No container binding, service provider, or bootstrap registration. No test double under `app/Foundation`. No concrete identifier subclass. No new exception type. No PHPStan level raise, baseline, or suppression, and no Pint rule, exclusion, or preset change. **No change to any existing test file other than the owner-authorized directory-inventory expectation in `EntityTest.php`, and no existing test, assertion, or security control removed or weakened anywhere.** No business module, no `app/Modules`, no production deployment, no new dependency.

## Module Infrastructure
- PF-060 through PF-063

### PF-064 — Module Migration Registration — Done

**Identifier assigned by the technical owner on 13 August 2026.** The dependency analysis below is retained ahead of the contract because it is what justifies minting a new identifier rather than reusing an existing name; deleting it would leave `PF-064` looking arbitrary.

**Why this analysis exists.** An earlier `PA-007` draft asserted that `PF-060` Module Loader, `PF-061` Module Service Provider, and `PF-063` Module Registration were **all three** mandatory prerequisites for discovering and executing a module-owned migration. **That assertion was manufactured and is withdrawn.** Independent review raised it as a P1: a three-story chain would serialize the critical path on stories whose scope no accepted contract has fixed.

**What the repository actually says.** `PF-060` through `PF-063` exist as **names only**, in exactly two places — the Sprint 0.4 list in `docs/implementation/01_Implementation_Sprint_Plan.md` and the one-line entry above. **No accepted story contract, ADR, or architecture document assigns migration discovery or execution to any of them, or apportions it among them.** `docs/adr/ADR-020-Migration-Rollback-and-Schema-Evolution.md` Decision 11 and `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §5.1 require a module's migrations to live in its own `Database/` directory; **neither names a mechanism or a story.** `docs/domain/06_Laravel_Module_Blueprint.md` shows `Database/` and `ModuleServiceProvider.php` in the standard module template, but `PA-001` already established — and `app/Modules/PlatformAdministration/README.md` records — that **the template is not an instruction to create empty directories**, so its shape proves nothing about which story must land first.

**The minimum indispensable behaviour is one capability, not three.** Laravel discovers migrations outside `database/migrations` through exactly one mechanism: a registered service provider calling `loadMigrationsFrom()`, which defers to `Migrator::path()` and appends to the migrator's path list. Every `migrate`, `migrate:fresh`, `migrate:rollback`, and `migrate:status` invocation, and every test-suite migration run, consults that list. **Therefore the sole indispensable behaviour is: register each approved module's migration directory with the migrator, once, deterministically, at boot.**

**Assessed story by story, against indispensability rather than name adjacency:**

| Story | Indispensable for module-migration discovery? | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `PF-060` Module Loader | **No.** | Nothing needs loading. `app/Modules` is already covered by the existing `App\` PSR-4 mapping, and `PA-001` landed six module classes with **no autoload, namespace, or Composer change** — recorded in its own accepted contract. Migration-path registration reads directory paths from disk and resolves no module class at all. |
| `PF-061` Module Service Provider | **No — and deliberately not redefined.** | The capability *is* delivered by a service provider, so a story of this name could plausibly have contained it. But `docs/domain/06_Laravel_Module_Blueprint.md` places `ModuleServiceProvider.php` **inside each module**, and satisfying `PA-007` that way would force creating `app/Modules/PlatformAdministration/ModuleServiceProvider.php` — per-module scaffolding that `PlatformAdministrationContractShapeTest` currently asserts absent. A **single shared, application-level** provider needs no such file. Whether `PF-061` means the per-module provider or a shared registration provider **is recorded nowhere**, so assigning the capability to it would silently redefine a story that has a name but no accepted contract. |
| `PF-062` Module Generator | **No.** | Already recorded above as **not required** by Release 0.1. Nothing is scaffolded or generated. |
| `PF-063` Module Registration | **No — and deliberately not redefined.** | Registering migration paths is literally a form of module registration, so a story of this name could plausibly have contained it. But "module registration" in its general sense also covers routes, configuration, container bindings, commands, and event wiring — **none of which `PA-007` needs**, and requiring all of it would be exactly the over-serialization this analysis withdrew. Narrowing `PF-063` to a migration-path slice would likewise redefine a story that has a name but no accepted contract. |

**Conclusion: the smallest genuine prerequisite is a single narrow capability, and no existing identifier demonstrably owned it.** `PF-060` and `PF-062` are excluded on evidence. `PF-061` and `PF-063` were each plausible homes, but assigning to either would have silently changed what that identifier means.

**Recorded technical-owner decision, 13 August 2026 — a new identifier, `PF-064`.** The narrow capability is assigned to **`PF-064` — Module Migration Registration**. **`PF-060`, `PF-061`, `PF-062`, and `PF-063` are not renumbered, renamed, merged, split, deleted, or redefined**; they remain names reserved for their own later independent analysis. Minting a new identifier costs nothing, keeps those four free to be scoped on their own merits, and **limits the 31 August critical path to the single capability `PA-007` actually needs.**

#### Story contract

**Status.** `Done` — merged to `main` on 17 August 2026. The repository owner separately authorized implementation on 14 August 2026 under this accepted contract and its seven-file allowlist. The implementation adds one shared `ModuleMigrationServiceProvider`, one bootstrap registration, and focused unit and PostgreSQL feature evidence; it adds no business migration, schema, model, repository, module loader, manifest, route, binding, command, bus, or deployment.

**Verified merge evidence.** Reviewed head **`a4db849e27a91aae229da3e16cbbc5d77c48a010`**, merged by pull request **#62** as merge commit **`bb7f94a367ad19d248aff205bbcce2e49928d630`**, which is the current tip of `main`. All **seven** approved paths were confirmed byte-identical between the reviewed head and merged `main`, and the reviewed head is an ancestor of `main`. All four required protected checks — `PHP Code Quality`, `Frontend Build`, `Application Tests`, `Dependency Audit` — passed on that exact head, and the required human approval is recorded on #62. Independent review closed with **no unresolved P0, P1, or P2**; three residual P3 findings were recorded and explicitly accepted by the owner at approval, and none is required by this Definition of Done. The canonical PHP 8.4 / PostgreSQL 16 run, rebased onto `main` with PF-074 merged and provisioned with its three separated roles, the database guard armed, passed with **663 tests and 2,411 assertions**; the focused PF-064 suites passed with **4 tests and 26 assertions**; Pint passed across **72 files** and PHPStan level 5 passed across **45 files**. These totals supersede the pre-rebase figures of 643 / 2,215 and Pint 70 files: merging PF-074 added its two suites and two files to the baseline. The implementation branch and worktree are removed locally and remotely, and no disposable container, volume, database, role, fixture artifact, credential, or secret remains.

**Done makes no claim** that any module migration, relation, database role, `PA-007` deliverable, tenant isolation, or Release 0.1 capability exists, and **nothing is deployed**. PF-064 delivers migration *discovery and registration* only.

**Objective.** Make migrations owned by an approved module under `app/Modules/<Module>/Database/Migrations` discoverable and executable by Laravel's ordinary migration commands and by the test suite, **without placing any module migration in `database/migrations`** and without coupling shared infrastructure to any business module.

**Owner.** Platform Foundation. The capability governs the whole application's migration path list, so it is platform infrastructure and not any bounded context's — the same reasoning `docs/adr/ADR-020-Migration-Rollback-and-Schema-Evolution.md` Decision 11 applies to platform infrastructure relations.

**PF-064 owns exactly five things:**

- **deterministic discovery** of approved module migration directories;
- **registration** of those directories with Laravel's migrator;
- **globally unique migration-basename enforcement**;
- **compatibility** with ordinary Laravel migration commands and tests;
- **the one shared application-level provider and its one bootstrap registration.**

**PF-064 owns none of the following, and must not stub, approximate, or partially implement any of them:** module loading; general module registration; per-module service providers; module manifests or lifecycle; module generation or scaffolding; routes; configuration; bindings; commands; event listeners; command, query, handler, or event buses; and any business migration, schema, model, repository, or relation.

**Exact scope — one provider and its registration:**

- One application-level service provider, `App\Providers\ModuleMigrationServiceProvider`, that resolves every existing `app/Modules/*/Database/Migrations` directory **by glob, never by a hardcoded module name**, and registers each through `loadMigrationsFrom()`.
- Registration of that provider **once** in `bootstrap/providers.php`.
- **Deterministic ordering** — the resolved directory list is canonicalized and sorted before registration, and module migration filenames keep the standard timestamp prefix, so `Migrator::getMigrationFiles()`'s sort-by-migration-name yields one stable global order across all paths.
- **Duplicate prevention, at both levels.** `Migrator::path()` already de-duplicates identical paths through `array_unique`; the provider additionally canonicalizes each path so two spellings of one directory cannot both register. Separately, **`Migrator::getMigrationFiles()` keys files by migration name, so two migrations sharing a basename across different paths silently collapse to one** — the contract therefore requires a guard test asserting that every discovered migration basename is globally unique. **This is a correctness hazard, not a formality**: a collapsed migration is a relation that silently never ships.
- **Absent directories are skipped silently**, so a module with no relations neither breaks boot nor requires an empty directory.

**Explicit exclusions.** No module loader, module registry, module manifest, or module lifecycle. **No per-module `ModuleServiceProvider.php`, and no empty module scaffolding of any kind.** No module generator or `make:` command. No route file, configuration file, container binding, command bus, query bus, handler registration, event bus, or listener registration. No business-module import, reference, or hardcoded module name in shared infrastructure. **No change to `phpunit.xml`, `.github/workflows/`, `composer.json`, `composer.lock`, or any Docker or environment file**, and **no weakening of `PF-033`** — the PostgreSQL engine and non-superuser/`NOBYPASSRLS` guard passes byte-identical, and the four required `Protect main` check names are unchanged. No relation, schema, migration, or seeder of its own, and **no committed production file under `app/Modules/**` is created** — the feature suite's ephemeral, pre-boot fixture is the sole exception, exists only for the duration of one test, is removed unconditionally, and is never committed. **It does not create, own, or run `PA-007`'s migration**; it makes such a migration discoverable and nothing more.

**Dependencies.** `PF-033` PostgreSQL Continuous Integration — **Done**; its `REQUIRE_POSTGRESQL_TEST_DATABASE` gate is the mechanism the feature suite reuses unchanged. Approved architecture: ADR-020 Decision 11, `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §5.1, `docs/domain/06_Laravel_Module_Blueprint.md`. **`PF-064` has no code dependency on `PF-074`, on `PA-007`, on `PF-060`–`PF-063`, or on any IdentityAccess story.**

**Dependents.** `PA-007`, which cannot place a migration in its module `Database/` directory until this exists. `PF-064` is not made Ready, scheduled, or authorized by `PA-007`'s contract, and does not become `PA-007`'s subordinate.

**Acceptance criteria.** A future `PF-064` implementation is correct only if all of the following hold:

1. Exactly one new production file exists, `app/Providers/ModuleMigrationServiceProvider.php`, registered exactly once in `bootstrap/providers.php`.
2. Discovery is **glob-based**; the provider contains **no business-module name**, asserted by scanning its source.
3. Resolved paths are **canonicalized and sorted**, and registration is **idempotent** across repeated boots and repeated provider registration.
4. A migration placed under `app/Modules/<Module>/Database/Migrations` **is discovered** by the migrator, asserted against the migrator's resolved paths and file list.
5. **Every discovered migration basename is globally unique**, asserted across all registered paths plus `database/migrations`.
6. A module with no `Database/Migrations` directory **does not break boot** and requires no empty directory.
7. **No migration is added to `database/migrations`**, and **no committed production file under `app/Modules/**` is created**. The feature suite's ephemeral pre-boot fixture is the sole permitted transient exception (criterion 12) and is never committed.
8. Ordinary `migrate`, `migrate:fresh`, `migrate:rollback`, and `migrate:status` and the test-suite migration run all see the registered paths.
9. `tests/Feature/PostgreSqlTestDatabaseGuardTest.php`, the `PF-049` Foundation guard, both `FirmTransactionManager` suites, and the `PA-001` suites pass **byte-identical and unchanged**.
10. `phpunit.xml`, `phpstan.neon.dist`, `pint.json`, `.github/**`, `.githooks/**`, `composer.*`, `package*`, `config/database.php`, and Docker and environment files are unchanged, and no dependency is added.
11. The four required `Protect main` check names are unchanged.
12. **The feature fixture is arranged before boot and removed unconditionally.** It is created in `setUp()` **before** `parent::setUp()`; the test application boots afterwards and discovers it through the **production glob**, not through any test-only hook, environment variable, provider option, configuration key, manifest, module registry, or hardcoded module name; it is removed in `tearDown()` before `parent::tearDown()` even when an assertion or command fails; **after removal no fixture file and no fixture directory remains**, asserted; `app/Modules/PlatformAdministration` is neither read nor modified; and **`git status` is clean after the suite runs**, so no fixture is ever committed.
13. Every command family in criterion 8 is proved against the registered fixture — `migrate` applies it, `migrate:status` reports it as `Ran`, `migrate:rollback` reverses it, `migrate:fresh` rediscovers and reapplies it — and the suite leaves the database **fully migrated**, applying no ambient-transaction trait.

**Focused test plan.**

- *`tests/Unit/Providers/ModuleMigrationServiceProviderTest.php`* — a pure unit suite proving glob-based discovery against a **temporary fixture directory tree** rather than any production module; canonicalization and sort order; idempotent registration; silent skipping of absent directories; and that the provider source references no business-module name. Fixtures are created and removed inside the test.
- *`tests/Feature/Providers/ModuleMigrationServiceProviderPostgreSqlTest.php`* — a gated PostgreSQL suite using the existing `PF-033` `REQUIRE_POSTGRESQL_TEST_DATABASE` pattern (`addToAssertionCount(1)` and return when unarmed, never `markTestSkipped`), proving **all four ordinary migration command families and the ordinary test migration lifecycle** against a registered ephemeral fixture, and that **every discovered migration basename is globally unique**. It applies no ambient-transaction trait — not `RefreshDatabase`, `DatabaseTransactions`, or `DatabaseMigrations` — for the same reason `PF-073`'s PostgreSQL suite does not: the commands under test manage their own transactions.

**Required command coverage, matching acceptance criterion 8 exactly.** Acceptance criterion 8 is unchanged and unweakened; the feature suite must discharge every part of it against the registered fixture, in this order:

1. **`migrate`** applies the fixture migration, and the fixture relation exists in the catalogue afterwards.
2. **`migrate:status`** reports the fixture migration, by its migration name, as `Ran`.
3. **`migrate:rollback`** reverses it, and the fixture relation is absent from the catalogue afterwards.
4. **`migrate:fresh`** rediscovers and reapplies it from the registered path, and the fixture relation exists again afterwards.
5. **The ordinary test migration lifecycle** sees the registered path — asserted against the migrator's resolved paths as the test application resolves them, not against a re-implementation of discovery inside the test.

**`migrate:fresh` is destructive and the contract says so rather than hiding it.** It drops every relation in the database before re-running all migrations. That is acceptable only because this suite is gated on the armed flag and CI provisions a **disposable** database per job. The suite must therefore leave the database in a **fully migrated state**, must not depend on data any other suite created, and must not create data any other suite depends on. If a future change makes that untrue, the ordering — not the assertion — is what must change.

**Pre-boot fixture arrangement, cleanup, and the production/ephemeral distinction.**

The provider registers migration paths **during application boot**, so a fixture created inside a test method would arrive too late to be discovered. The narrowest reliable mechanism the existing infrastructure already supports is PHPUnit's own lifecycle, and the contract requires exactly that — **no production-only test hook, environment variable, provider constructor option, configuration key, manifest, module registry, or hardcoded module name may be introduced to make the test work.**

- **Before boot.** The suite overrides `setUp()`, and **before calling `parent::setUp()`** — which is what constructs and boots the application — it asserts the fixture path does not already exist (failing closed on a leaked fixture from an earlier run), then creates an ephemeral module-shaped directory and one timestamp-prefixed migration file beneath it.
- **Boot after the fixture exists.** `parent::setUp()` then boots a dedicated application instance for that test, so `ModuleMigrationServiceProvider` discovers the fixture **through the same production glob** every real module would be found by. The test asserts discovery against the booted application's migrator, never by re-implementing the glob.
- **Unconditional cleanup.** The suite overrides `tearDown()` and removes the fixture directory and file **before** calling `parent::tearDown()`. PHPUnit runs `tearDown()` after a failed assertion or a failed command, so cleanup is not conditional on the test passing; the removal is written to be safe when the fixture was never created.
- **Cleanup is verified.** After removal, `tearDown()` asserts that **no fixture file and no fixture directory remains**, so a partial cleanup fails the test rather than leaking into the next run or into the working tree.
- **The fixture never touches the real module.** It uses its own ephemeral module-shaped directory name and **never reads, writes, or modifies `app/Modules/PlatformAdministration`** or any file `PA-001` delivered.
- **Production versus ephemeral, stated explicitly.** **`PF-064`'s implementation creates no committed production file under `app/Modules` — that prohibition is unchanged and unweakened.** Its feature test may create an **ephemeral, module-shaped fixture inside the worktree for the duration of a single test only**, before application boot, and must remove it unconditionally. **No fixture migration or fixture directory is ever committed**, and `git status` after the suite must be clean.

**Full validation plan.** In the canonical Docker PHP 8.4 / PostgreSQL 16 environment, against a disposable non-superuser `NOBYPASSRLS` role and database per the documented `CONTRIBUTING.md` recipe, with the engine guard **armed**: `git diff --check`; `composer validate --strict`; `composer pint:test`; `composer phpstan` at level 5 with no baseline, suppression, or ignored error; both suites above; the unchanged `PF-033`, `PF-049`, `FirmTransactionManager`, and `PA-001` suites passing byte-identical; the complete PostgreSQL-backed suite passing with a recorded test and assertion count strictly above the measured pre-implementation baseline; and `composer audit --locked --abandoned=report` and `npm audit --audit-level=high` both clean. Independent review records no unresolved P0, P1, or P2.

**The four required `Protect main` checks remain exactly:** `PHP Code Quality`, `Frontend Build`, `Application Tests`, `Dependency Audit`.

**Allowed implementation files.** A future `PF-064` implementation — once this contract passes independent review **and** implementation is separately authorized — may create or change **only**:

- `app/Providers/ModuleMigrationServiceProvider.php`;
- `bootstrap/providers.php`;
- `tests/Unit/Providers/ModuleMigrationServiceProviderTest.php`;
- `tests/Feature/Providers/ModuleMigrationServiceProviderPostgreSqlTest.php`;
- `docs/implementation/03_Engineering_Backlog.md` and `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`, for verified lifecycle and evidence updates;
- `docs/implementation/01_Implementation_Sprint_Plan.md`, for the lifecycle note only.

Any additional source, test, provider, configuration, workflow, or database file requires a separately reviewed contract correction and explicit approval before editing.

**Forbidden files and changes.** No change to `database/migrations/**`, `database/seeders/**`, or `database/factories/**`; to any committed file under `app/Modules/**` — including every `PA-001` file, which the feature suite neither reads nor modifies — or under `app/Foundation/**` or `app/Infrastructure/**`; to `app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php`; to `config/database.php` or any other configuration file; to `phpunit.xml`, `phpstan.neon.dist`, or `pint.json`; to `.github/**`, `.githooks/**`, `composer.*`, `package*`, Docker, or environment files; or to any existing Foundation, Infrastructure, or `PlatformAdministration` test. **No architecture or ADR file may be edited by the implementation story.** **No required check, test, assertion, guard, static-analysis level, formatting rule, security control, or approval gate may be removed, weakened, renamed, skipped, or suppressed.**

**Definition of Ready.** **Met after this technical-owner decision and successful independent contract review.** Every other element — objective, owner, scope, exclusions, mechanism, ordering, duplicate handling, dependencies, acceptance criteria, tests, validation, allowed and forbidden files — is resolved here, and `PF-064`'s only dependency, `PF-033`, is `Done`. **Ready is not authorization**: implementation still requires its own explicit go-ahead.

**Definition of Done.** `PF-064` is Done only when: this contract has passed independent review and received recorded final technical acceptance; implementation is separately authorized; the contract is implemented strictly within its allowlist; every acceptance criterion passes; both suites pass, including the gated PostgreSQL run with the engine guard armed; the unchanged `PF-033`, `PF-049`, `FirmTransactionManager`, and `PA-001` suites pass byte-identical; Pint, PHPStan level 5, `composer validate --strict`, both dependency audits, and `git diff --check` pass; independent review reports no unresolved P0, P1, or P2; **all four required protected checks pass on the exact final implementation head**; the required human approval comment is recorded; the PR merges to `main`; tracking records only verified evidence; and the implementation branch and worktree are removed locally and remotely. **Done would make no claim** that any module migration, relation, database role, `PA-007` deliverable, tenant isolation, or Release 0.1 capability exists.

**Sequencing.** **`PF-064` and `PF-074` are independent prerequisites of `PA-007` and may proceed in parallel** — neither consumes the other's output. **`PA-007` depends on both being `Done`.** `PF-064` neither schedules nor is scheduled by `PA-007`, and **assigning the identifier schedules and authorizes nothing.**

## Application Framework
- PF-070 through PF-074

### PF-073 — Transaction Manager — Done

**Objective.** Provide the single application transaction boundary that consumes an already verified `FirmContext`, opens one explicit PostgreSQL transaction, establishes the Firm setting transaction-locally before protected database work, executes one callback, and commits or rolls back as a unit. PF-073 is a technical Platform Foundation runtime adapter; it is not a business bounded context and does not itself complete tenant isolation.

**Owner.** Platform Foundation.

**Dependencies.** PF-033 PostgreSQL Continuous Integration and PF-080 Firm Context are Done. ARCH-012 is Approved; `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` and ADR-016 are authoritative for transaction-scoped Firm context. PF-073 does not depend on PF-081 or PF-082: those later stories supply and adapt verified context to this boundary. PF-091 depends on PF-073 where state, audit, idempotency, and outbox facts must commit atomically, but PF-091 is not a PF-073 dependency.

**Approved technical decisions — independently reviewed and technically approved on 11 August 2026.**

1. The implementation is `App\Infrastructure\Database\FirmTransactionManager`, outside `app/Foundation` because it depends on Laravel's concrete database connection adapter. This is the approved PF-073 reconciliation of the older `app/Foundation/Persistence` placement in `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §24: the source root is new, but already covered by the existing `App\` PSR-4 mapping, and no Composer change is required. The framework-independent `FirmContext` remains under `App\Foundation\Tenancy`; no Laravel dependency is introduced into Foundation. “Platform Foundation owns” describes technical ownership and governance, not a requirement that framework-coupled code live in the `app/Foundation` directory.
2. The one platform-wide PostgreSQL setting is `onelegalpro.firm_id`, declared once as `public const FIRM_SETTING = 'onelegalpro.firm_id'` on `FirmTransactionManager`. The dot is required for a PostgreSQL custom setting; the qualified OneLegalPro prefix avoids collision with PostgreSQL and third-party settings. No module repeats or replaces this string.
3. The public operation is `run(FirmContext $context, callable $callback): mixed`, with the exact PHPStan contract `@template TResult`, `@param callable(Connection): TResult $callback`, and `@return TResult`, where `Connection` is imported from `Illuminate\Database\Connection`. This Pint-compatible spelling denotes the same approved concrete Laravel type; it does not weaken or abstract the callback contract. PF-073 invokes the callback with the exact managed connection, binding callback database work to the connection carrying the local setting. The callback receives no scalar Firm identifier and no mutable context; it executes only after the exact supplied context has established the transaction setting.
4. The adapter uses parameter-bound `SELECT set_config(?, ?, true)` rather than interpolated `SET LOCAL`. The third argument `true` gives the transaction-local semantics equivalent to `SET LOCAL`; parameter binding prevents a setting value from becoming SQL text. `set_config(..., false)` and session-level `SET` are prohibited.
5. PF-073 rejects every ambient transaction before opening its own, whether managed or unmanaged and whether it would use the same or a different Firm. Same-Firm nesting, savepoint nesting, joining an existing transaction, different-Firm re-entry, and manager-mediated context replacement all fail closed. This deliberately keeps one manager call equal to one outer transaction, makes setting establishment occur exactly once, and avoids Laravel savepoints turning an inner rollback into a still-live outer transaction with ambiguous ownership.
6. On success, PF-073 commits and returns the callback's exact result. On a callback or setting failure, it attempts rollback and cleanup verification and, when both succeed, rethrows the exact original instance. A rollback or cleanup-verification failure is not hidden in order to preserve the callback throwable: it raises `\RuntimeException` with the original failure chained as evidence, because connection state is then unknown. A cleanup-verification failure after a successful commit must use a message that explicitly states that the transaction **committed durably but connection cleanup could not be verified**; callers must not retry it as if commit had failed. A rollback-path infrastructure failure must explicitly state that commit did not complete. `\RuntimeException` is deliberately not a machine-readable retry discriminator because PF-073 permits no automatic retry; any future retry facility must introduce separately approved typed outcome classification rather than parsing messages or treating every `\RuntimeException` alike. Commit, rollback, cleanup, or connection failures are never converted into a success or a domain result.
7. Read callbacks use the same explicit transaction boundary as writes. Autocommit reads of Firm-scoped relations are prohibited.
8. Automatic retry is disabled. A later separately approved facility may retry a serialization failure or deadlock only for a command proved idempotent or protected by ADR-021 idempotency persistence.
9. The transaction contains no external HTTP, provider, email, messaging, object-storage, search-index, AI/model, queue-push, user wait, sleep, or polling operation. PF-073 cannot infer callback contents; this is a caller contract reinforced by review and owning-story tests, not an overstated runtime guarantee.
10. PF-073 establishes only the transaction-local Firm identifier. The Actor and correlation identifiers remain available through the exact `FirmContext` supplied to the caller's application layer; they are not additional PostgreSQL settings in this story.
11. Before opening the transaction, PF-073 verifies that the checked-out connection is not a superuser, has no `BYPASSRLS`, and carries no non-empty pre-existing `onelegalpro.firm_id` session value. Every scalar query used for preflight, setting establishment, in-transaction verification, and post-transaction cleanup passes `false` as `Connection::scalar()`'s third argument so Laravel always uses the write PDO; the adapter never permits a read-PDO checkout to verify state different from the connection that owns the transaction. A failing role check or leaked session value causes the connection to be disconnected and the operation to fail before callback execution. This is connection quarantine, not a `RESET`-based recovery claim. Relation ownership and grants remain schema/deployment controls tested by their owning stories; database ownership is not used as their proxy, and PF-033's current test role may own its disposable test database while remaining non-superuser and without `BYPASSRLS`.
12. After callback execution and again after transaction end, PF-073 verifies the expected setting state. A callback that leaves a changed Firm value is rejected before commit. Direct `SET`, `SET LOCAL`, `set_config`, or equivalent Firm-setting writes outside this adapter are prohibited and guarded by a production-source scan. Because arbitrary dynamically assembled SQL cannot be made impossible by a PHP adapter, the contract does not claim PF-073 can detect a transient direct switch that a malicious callback restores before returning; review, the single-connection callback contract, least-privilege code ownership, and the source guard enforce that prohibition alongside the runtime checks.

**Proposed implementation contract.** Create one final adapter at `app/Infrastructure/Database/FirmTransactionManager.php`. It receives one `Illuminate\Database\Connection` through its constructor and exposes only the setting-name constant and `run()`. The concrete Laravel connection is intentional: PF-073 must pass the exact managed connection into the callback and must be able to quarantine a contaminated checkout through `disconnect()`, neither of which is an optional concern hidden behind a narrower contract. Before beginning, `run()` verifies that the connection transaction level is zero; otherwise it throws `\LogicException` before invoking the callback or issuing the setting statement — an ambient transaction is a programming/adapter contract violation, not a domain failure, so PF-049's domain exceptions are not misused.

Still before the managed transaction, PF-073 uses parameter-bound scalar queries with `$useReadPdo = false` to verify the current PostgreSQL role is not superuser, has no `BYPASSRLS`, and that `nullif(current_setting(?, true), '')` is `NULL` for the canonical Firm setting. It never trusts a checkout merely because the application created it. A failed role check, an indeterminate result, or a non-empty pre-existing setting disconnects the connection and throws before callback execution; PF-073 never attempts to normalize contamination with session-level `SET`, `set_config(..., false)`, or a best-effort `RESET`.

It then begins the transaction, binds the canonical setting name and `$context->firmId()->toString()` to `SELECT set_config(?, ?, true)` through `Connection::scalar(..., false)`, and verifies that the returned scalar exactly equals the supplied canonical Firm identifier before invoking `$callback($connection)`. The callback and every repository it invokes must use that passed connection; reaching for a facade, another named connection, or an independently resolved connection inside the callback is prohibited and must be covered by each consuming story's integration tests. PF-073 does not falsely claim PHP can make arbitrary global connection access unrepresentable. Immediately after the callback, the current transaction-local value must still equal the original Firm identifier or PF-073 rolls back, disconnects, and raises. After successful commit or rollback, the same write connection must report the setting as absent or unusably empty through `Connection::scalar(..., false)`; otherwise it is disconnected and a `\RuntimeException` is raised with the committed/not-committed outcome stated explicitly. A failure to establish the setting rolls back and raises; because `FirmContext` already requires typed, non-null identifiers, PF-073 has no separate “missing context” input path, while an empty, malformed, unconfirmed, or contaminated database setting still fails before protected callback execution.

The exact supplied `FirmContext` is not reconstructed, mutated, cached, serialized, logged, or treated as authorization. Possessing it proves no membership, entitlement, role, permission, Ethical Wall outcome, or resource access. IdentityAccess remains the authority for verified identity and membership; PF-081/PF-082 own their later verified handoff and ingress composition; each business domain still owns current resource authorization and Practice Management alone owns Ethical Wall decisions.

**Acceptance criteria.** One explicit transaction is opened only from transaction level zero; connection role and pre-existing Firm session state are verified before it begins; contaminated or privileged connections are disconnected and rejected; the canonical Firm setting is established transaction-locally and parameter-bound before the callback; the callback receives the exact managed connection; its exact result is returned after commit; the exact original throwable is rethrown when rollback and cleanup verification succeed, while rollback or cleanup failure is surfaced with the original failure preserved; the setting is absent or unusably empty on the reused connection after both commit and rollback; an un-restored context change is rejected before commit; reads receive the same boundary as writes; every ambient or nested manager transaction is rejected before callback execution; production code outside the adapter contains no direct Firm-setting statement; no automatic retry or external call is introduced; and no Foundation, schema, RLS, role, grant, migration, resolver, middleware, module, repository, outbox, audit, or idempotency implementation is added.

**Allowed implementation files.** A future PF-073 implementation may change only:

- `app/Infrastructure/Database/FirmTransactionManager.php`;
- `tests/Unit/Infrastructure/Database/FirmTransactionManagerTest.php`;
- `tests/Feature/Infrastructure/Database/FirmTransactionManagerPostgreSqlTest.php`, guarded by the existing PF-033 `REQUIRE_POSTGRESQL_TEST_DATABASE` mechanism so ordinary SQLite runs skip it while CI fails closed if PostgreSQL coverage is not executed;
- `docs/implementation/03_Engineering_Backlog.md` and `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` for verified lifecycle/evidence updates;
- `app/Foundation/README.md` only to record the implemented adapter boundary without placing database code under Foundation.

Any additional source, test, provider, configuration, workflow, or database file requires a separately reviewed contract correction and explicit approval before editing.

**Forbidden files and changes.** No change to `app/Foundation/Tenancy/FirmContext.php`, any existing Foundation source/test, `composer.json`, `composer.lock`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `phpunit.xml`, `phpstan.neon.dist`, `pint.json`, `.github/`, `.githooks/`, Docker/environment files, Laravel bootstrap/configuration, service providers, routes, controllers, middleware, jobs, migrations, schema, RLS policies, PostgreSQL roles/grants/functions, modules, repositories, outbox, audit, or idempotency persistence. No required check, assertion, static-analysis level, formatting rule, security control, or PostgreSQL CI guard may be removed, weakened, renamed, skipped, or suppressed.

**Required tests.** The focused unit suite extends plain `PHPUnit\Framework\TestCase`; it must not use `RefreshDatabase`, `DatabaseTransactions`, or any ambient-transaction trait because Decision 5 requires every manager call to start at transaction level zero. It must prove the exact final class, namespace, concrete `Connection` constructor dependency, constant name/value, literal PHPStan generic callback contract, single public operation, no extra API, transaction-level-zero guard, role/session preflight, contaminated-connection disconnect, begin/setting/callback/commit order, the exact connection passed to the callback, exact result preservation, successful rollback with exact original throwable propagation, rollback-failure `\RuntimeException` reporting with the original failure preserved, post-commit cleanup failure explicitly reporting that durable commit occurred, setting-establishment failure before callback, post-callback value verification, post-commit/post-rollback cleanup verification, same-Firm and different-Firm nesting rejection through the universal ambient-transaction rule, no join/savepoint path, and no automatic retry. It must prove every SQL call uses `Connection::scalar()` with bound parameters and `$useReadPdo = false`, uses `set_config(..., true)`, and never interpolates the Firm value or uses session-level `SET` or `set_config(..., false)`. A source guard scans production PHP and fails if the canonical setting literal or a Firm-setting `SET`/`set_config` statement exists outside `FirmTransactionManager.php`; the guard is a review control and makes no claim to solve arbitrary dynamically assembled SQL. The suite also proves the adapter itself imports and invokes no external client; arbitrary callback behavior remains an owning-story review and integration-test obligation because PF-073 cannot reliably infer it at runtime.

The PostgreSQL feature suite must use the existing PF-033 `REQUIRE_POSTGRESQL_TEST_DATABASE` gate exactly as `PostgreSqlTestDatabaseGuardTest.php` does: ordinary local SQLite execution records the guard assertion and returns without PostgreSQL-only work, while the existing CI guard fails closed if CI does not use PostgreSQL, so no workflow or `phpunit.xml` change is needed. Neither this feature suite nor any helper it uses may apply `RefreshDatabase`, `DatabaseTransactions`, or another ambient-transaction trait. Under the PF-033 non-superuser, `NOBYPASSRLS` PostgreSQL 16 test role it proves: role preflight passes for that runtime test role; a seeded non-empty session-level Firm setting is detected before callback, the connection is disconnected, and no leaked value is reused; the setting is visible inside the managed callback; the callback receives the exact connection; a read callback receives the same boundary; the exact setting value matches the supplied Firm identifier; another manager entry cannot change it; a direct un-restored callback change is detected before commit; it is absent or unusably empty after commit on the same reused connection; it is absent or unusably empty after rollback on the same reused connection; callback work commits on success and rolls back on failure using test-owned temporary evidence without adding production schema; and an ambient transaction fails before callback work. Privileged-role rejection and disconnect behavior are proved in the focused suite with a controlled connection test double; PF-073 does not authorize CI role creation or workflow changes. Empty/populated RLS-relation proofs remain mandatory for the later schema/RLS story and PF-082 integration but are not falsely claimed by PF-073, which creates no policy or Firm-scoped relation.

The full suite, focused suites, unchanged Foundation guard, PF-033 PostgreSQL engine/role guard, Pint, PHPStan level 5, `composer validate --strict`, Composer audit, npm audit, and `git diff --check` must pass. The four required `Protect main` check names remain exactly `PHP Code Quality`, `Frontend Build`, `Application Tests`, and `Dependency Audit`.

**Security.** A client-supplied Firm or Actor identifier never establishes context. No header, route, parameter, body, cookie, hostname, custom domain, or email is accepted. The canonical setting is transaction-local and must not survive commit or rollback. Manager-mediated switching is impossible; direct setting writes outside the adapter are prohibited, guarded, and not falsely claimed to be unrepresentable in arbitrary SQL. Connection role and session state are verified on the write PDO rather than trusted; a privileged or contaminated checkout is disconnected and fails before protected callback work. Consistent with ADR-016 Decision 9 and `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §3.4, PF-073's pre-statement application enforcement is where the fail-closed guarantee lives; PostgreSQL row policy is the independent defence-in-depth layer. PF-073 is not authentication, membership verification, authorization, repository scoping, RLS, or an Ethical Wall.

**Definition of Ready (met).** The objective, dependencies, responsibility boundary, namespace/API, canonical setting, parameter-binding rule, nesting/ambient-transaction rule, retry rule, allowlist, exclusions, tests, and security implications are approved above. Independent review and two focused confirmation reviews resolved every reported finding, leaving no P0, P1, P2, or P3. The technical-owner approval comment was recorded on PR #51; all four required protected checks passed on exact final head `9f6ffbf`; and the contract PR merged to `main` as `c2b7549` on 11 August 2026. Readiness was met before implementation began; implementation runs on its separately authorized implementation branch and remains confined to the approved allowlist.

Three files were created and nothing else was touched: `app/Infrastructure/Database/FirmTransactionManager.php`, `tests/Unit/Infrastructure/Database/FirmTransactionManagerTest.php`, and `tests/Feature/Infrastructure/Database/FirmTransactionManagerPostgreSqlTest.php`. No existing file was modified apart from this tracking entry, `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`, and the allowlisted `app/Foundation/README.md`; no existing test, assertion, static-analysis level, formatting rule, security control, or PostgreSQL CI guard was removed, weakened, renamed, skipped, or suppressed. The technical owner approved the imported `Connection` spelling on 12 August 2026 after Pint demonstrated that it normalizes the fully qualified docblock spelling; the imported form denotes the identical concrete type and requires no formatter exception.

Validated in the canonical Docker PHP 8.4 / PostgreSQL 16 environment against a disposable non-superuser `NOBYPASSRLS` role and database, created by the documented `CONTRIBUTING.md` recipe: `git diff --check` clean; `composer validate --strict` valid; Pint passed across 58 files; PHPStan level 5 clean across 38 files with no baseline, suppression, or ignored error; the focused unit suite passed (42 tests, 335 assertions); the focused PostgreSQL feature suite passed under the armed `REQUIRE_POSTGRESQL_TEST_DATABASE` gate (12 tests, 44 assertions) and recorded the guard assertion without PostgreSQL-only work on the ordinary SQLite path (12 tests, 16 assertions); the complete PostgreSQL-backed suite passed (**480 tests, 1,581 assertions**, up from the 426/1,202 baseline measured on `a2b1ee9` in the same environment); the PF-049 Foundation dependency guard passed byte-identical and unchanged (8 tests, 287 assertions); the PF-033 PostgreSQL engine/role guard passed unchanged (1 test, 8 assertions); and `composer audit --locked --abandoned=report` and `npm audit --audit-level=high` were both clean. Independent review additionally corrected four hardening gaps before staging: a failed `beginTransaction()` now disconnects and reports that no commit occurred; rollback failure while abandoning a setting/context-verification failure is no longer hidden; Laravel's silent reconnect during `BEGIN` is detected by comparing the verified and transactional raw PDO handles and rejected before callback work; and the exact superuser/BYPASSRLS probe SQL is pinned by focused tests.

**Approved implementation-time contract correction.** The stock Laravel Pint preset's `fully_qualified_strict_types` rule normalizes the fully qualified callback docblock type to the imported spelling `@param callable(Connection): TResult $callback`. On 12 August 2026 the technical owner approved that spelling together with `use Illuminate\Database\Connection;`: it denotes the identical concrete Laravel type, preserves PHPStan's contract, changes no runtime behavior, and avoids weakening `pint.json` or any required check. The adapter and exact contract test use that approved spelling.

**Completion evidence.** Final independent confirmation reviewed exact implementation head `d8d7a6d3264d63cca4d6ceda3d73ff8d337e576e`, found no P0, P1, or P2 finding, and required no correction. All four required protected checks — `PHP Code Quality`, `Frontend Build`, `Application Tests`, and `Dependency Audit` — passed on that exact head. After synchronization, the isolated Docker stack, implementation worktree, and local and remote implementation branches were removed. **Definition of Done.** The approved contract is implemented only within its final allowlist; every acceptance criterion and focused/PostgreSQL test passes; the complete canonical PostgreSQL suite and all static/format/dependency gates pass; no P0/P1 remains after independent review; all four protected checks pass on the exact final implementation head; required human approval is recorded; the implementation PR merges; tracking records only verified evidence; and its branch/worktree are removed locally and remotely. Done makes no claim that PF-081, PF-082, Firm schema, application-runtime roles, RLS policies, raising policy helpers, repositories, audit, idempotency, outbox, or a business module is implemented.

**Accepted cost.** The fail-closed checks add approximately five small PostgreSQL round trips to each managed transaction, including read-only transactions. Release 0.1 accepts that cost in exchange for verified role, setting establishment, unchanged context, and cleanup; optimization may occur only through a separately reviewed change that preserves those guarantees.

**Explicitly excluded.** Firm discovery/resolution; FirmContext construction; identity, membership, entitlement, session, role, permission, capability, authorization, Ethical Wall, or support-access decisions; HTTP/queue/CLI middleware; context switching; nested transactions or savepoints; retry/backoff; isolation-level selection; row/advisory locking; external calls; events/outbox; audit/idempotency writes; persistence repositories; Eloquent models/casts; schema/migrations; RLS policies/functions; database roles/grants; connection-pool configuration; logging/telemetry; business modules; production deployment.

### PF-074 — PostgreSQL Runtime Role Separation — Done

**Status.** `Done` — merged to `main` on 16 August 2026. The owner authorized implementation under this accepted contract and its file allowlist on 14 August 2026. Recording and approving a contract did not start the story—the separate database-design and security/authorization approval did. The `PF-074` identifier was assigned by the technical owner on 13 August 2026; **assigning an identifier schedules and authorizes nothing**, and **no `PF-*` story is added, renamed, renumbered, merged, split, deleted, or rescheduled** by this implementation.

**Verified merge evidence.** Reviewed head **`7032e080757e72666890c5bf7c66070550c6fafb`**, merged by pull request **#61** as merge commit **`835e04f10a171bb13cb3bcb8ea6431cbc5ee4c1a`**; both are ancestors of the current `main`. The **thirteen** approved paths were confirmed byte-identical between the reviewed head and merged `main` at merge time; the ten non-tracking paths remain byte-identical today, and the three shared tracking documents have since been further edited only by the later `PF-064` and IA-001/IA-002 merges and by this tracking pass. All four required protected checks — `PHP Code Quality`, `Frontend Build`, `Application Tests`, `Dependency Audit` — passed on that exact head, and the required human approval is recorded on #61. Independent review closed with **no unresolved P0, P1, or P2**: the earlier `P2-7` was withdrawn as a misattribution of technical decision 3, and `P2-2` was resolved as stale review bookkeeping. The implementation branch and worktree are removed locally and remotely, and no disposable container, volume, database, role, credential, or secret remains.

**Done makes no claim** that `PA-007`, `PF-091`, `PF-092`, `PF-081`, `PF-082`, any Firm-scoped relation, any Row-Level Security policy, the Firm registry, the outbox relation, audit persistence, idempotency, a business module, a production environment, a production credential, or any deployment exists. **Nothing is deployed.** The accepted object-creation exposure window stands as recorded: a function or type created by the migration role is executable or usable by `PUBLIC` until the `grants` phase next runs. That is a **known, accepted limitation, not a closed control**, and Done does not represent it otherwise.

**Implementation evidence (16 August 2026, after narrow correction and the owner's Option A decision).** The authorized implementation separates the migration, request-serving, and outbox roles in local Compose and the required `Application Tests` job; uses the tracked two-phase platform bootstrap migration; runs Laravel migrations through the named migration connection; grants the runtime role DML on **exactly the six framework tables technical decision 7 enumerates** and `USAGE`/`SELECT` on **only the two sequences owned by a column of one of those six** (`jobs_id_seq`, `failed_jobs_id_seq`); revokes `PUBLIC`, runtime, and outbox access to every function, routine, and standalone type in schema `public`; and adds PostgreSQL catalogue and configuration tests. The `grants` phase emits **8** grant statements — 6 tables plus 2 sequences. The canonical PostgreSQL 16 suite passes at **659 tests and 2,384 assertions**, including the unchanged PF-033 guard and both PF-073 suites, which pass byte-identical at **55 tests and 393 assertions**; the focused PF-074 suites pass at **20 tests and 196 assertions**. Pint passes across **69 files**, PHPStan level 5 reports no errors across **44 files**, `composer validate --strict`, Composer audit, npm audit, and `git diff --check` all pass. Re-running the complete provisioning flow leaves an identical ownership and ACL fingerprint. These are local pre-approval results, not protected-check, merge, deployment, or production-readiness claims.

**Narrow correction (14 August 2026).** Independent implementation review returned **READY FOR NARROW CORRECTION** with four P1 findings and no P0. All four are resolved strictly within the existing thirteen-file allowlist; **no fourteenth file was added, no accepted decision was changed, and no check, guard, assertion, or security boundary was weakened.**

- **Wildcard sequence grant.** The `grants` phase granted the runtime role `USAGE`/`SELECT` on **every** sequence in schema `public` via `relkind = 'S'`, which included `public.migrations_id_seq` — review demonstrated the request-serving role advancing it — and would have auto-granted every future business sequence. Sequence privileges are now derived from `pg_depend` ownership: only a sequence owned by a column of an enumerated table is granted. Together with the owner's Option A decision below, the grants phase emits **8** grants (6 tables + 2 sequences) instead of 12, and the runtime role is refused `nextval` on `migrations_id_seq` with SQLSTATE `42501`.
- **Ineffective default privileges.** The six `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES … REVOKE` statements were behaviourally inert. Root cause, established by execution rather than inference: PostgreSQL computes an `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` result starting from an **empty** access control list, so a `REVOKE` removes nothing and the empty result is never stored; and at object-creation time the built-in `acldefault()` — which grants `PUBLIC` `EXECUTE` on functions and `USAGE` on types — is **merged into** whatever `pg_default_acl` holds, so even a stored entry cannot exclude `PUBLIC`. Neither issuing the statements under the migration role's own identity nor seeding an entry with a prior `GRANT` changes this. The revocation is therefore applied to the objects themselves in the `grants` phase and asserted behaviourally; see the correction recorded under *Privilege assertions*.
- **Unbounded privilege tests.** The feature suite asserted no upper bound, which is why both findings above passed CI. It now asserts the runtime role's reachable tables are **exactly** the enumerated list, with exactly the accepted DML; that its reachable sequences are exactly those owned by an enumerated table's column; that it holds nothing on `migrations` or `migrations_id_seq`, nothing on an arbitrary unapproved table or sequence, and no `TRUNCATE`, `REFERENCES`, or `TRIGGER` anywhere; that the outbox role has no schema `USAGE` and is refused a real `SELECT`; and that no function or standalone type in `public` is reachable by `PUBLIC`, the runtime role, or the outbox role. **Eight mutation probes** — arbitrary-table DML, arbitrary-sequence access, restored `PUBLIC` function execution, unapproved runtime and outbox default privileges, `SELECT` on `migrations`, `USAGE` on `migrations_id_seq`, and outbox schema `USAGE` — each turn the suite red and were each reverted.
- **README onboarding.** The documented first-run sequence never ran the `grants` phase, so a developer following it reached a runtime role with no table privileges and an application that could not serve a request, while the notes claimed a working queue. The `grants` step is now part of the sequence, carries no credential, and the queue note is corrected.

Also corrected: the runtime-DDL probe now requires SQLSTATE `42501` rather than any throwable and additionally probes `ALTER TABLE` and `TRUNCATE`; the outbox role's denial is proved by a refused statement as well as from the catalogue; the protected-check test captures **every** job-level `name:` value so a fifth check fails it; the password scan asserts that **every** tracked database password literal ends in `_dev_only` or `_test_only`, and the CI bootstrap literal was renamed to conform; the `.env`-ignored assertion now requires a real ignore rule rather than being satisfied by the `!.env.example` negation; role names and passwords are read by `psql` with `\getenv` and no longer appear in `argv`; the required `TEMPORARY` grant is commented with the PF-073 suite that needs it; and the stale `Backlog` status line, the inaccurate default-ACL claims, and the present-tense statements that describe `main` rather than this branch are all corrected.

**Owner decision of 16 August 2026 — the runtime table set is the six of technical decision 7, and `users` and `password_reset_tokens` are removed.** Verification against the contract text found that the implementation granted the runtime role `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE` on **eight** framework tables while decision 7 enumerates **six** and excludes `users` and `password_reset_tokens` by name. Decision 7 is byte-identical on `main`, on the reviewed implementation commit, and after the first narrow correction — the contract was never amended, and no ADR or architecture document enumerates these tables. The exclusion's stated rationale still held on inspection: no test or route outside the PF-074 suite itself referenced either table. The technical owner selected conformance over amendment, so the script's enumerated list and the feature suite's closed-set assertion are both reduced to the accepted six, and the two sequences those six own (`jobs_id_seq`, `failed_jobs_id_seq`) are the only sequences granted. **The accepted boundary is unchanged; the implementation now matches it.** This narrowed the runtime role's reach; it widened nothing.

**Withdrawn — the earlier `P2-7` finding rested on a misattribution.** A previous revision of this paragraph recorded that "technical decision 3 scoped relation-level grants to the reviewed Laravel migration that creates the relation" and that resolving the tension needed an owner-level amendment. **That statement was false and is withdrawn.** Decision 3 concerns **ownership only** — `ALTER DATABASE … OWNER`, `ALTER SCHEMA … OWNER`, and that the runtime and outbox roles own nothing — and contains no clause about relation-specific grants. The clause quoted was a paraphrase of **decision 6**, which assigns grants to the future module migrations that create their own relations and which this implementation respects by creating no relation. The framework-table grants are authorised outright by **decision 7**, which establishes the two-phase model precisely because default privileges grant nothing, and **decision 8** independently reconciles `03_Database_Design.md` §15.5 by classifying the script as the tracked platform bootstrap migration. **There is no conflict with decision 3, and no amendment is required.**

**`P2-2` is resolved as stale review bookkeeping.** The identifier appears exactly once in the independent review record — inside the `F1` acceptance-matrix row, which reads "see P1-2, P2-1, P2-2, P2-3, P3-2" — with no heading, no definition, and no other reference anywhere in that document; the review's P2 headings run 1, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, skipping 2. The review demonstrably renumbered during drafting (its P1-4 is annotated "Raised from P2 after execution disproved the milder reading"), so the `F1` list was left pointing at an identifier that no longer existed. **It is a dangling cross-reference, not a missing finding**, and the review record has been reconciled accordingly. No finding was invented and none was deleted.

**Remaining, recorded for a separate story — not defects of this implementation.** `docs/adr/ADR-023-…` and `docs/architecture/20_Deployment_Operations_Architecture.md` still cite `.env.example` credentials that no longer exist; both paths are forbidden to PF-074, so correcting them requires a separately reviewed contract. **Traceability:** decision 8 says Compose mounts the directory and names a `grant` mode; the implementation mounts the single file to `10-provision-roles.sh` and names the mode `grants`. Both deviations are benign, were present at review, and are recorded rather than changed. **Also recorded:** the review's `P3-1` claimed the `TEMPORARY` grant was undocumented in the contract; it is not — **decision 9** documents it in full and names the PF-073 suite requiring it. Only the review's second point, that the script itself was uncommented, was correct, and that is fixed.

**Objective.** Deliver the platform-wide PostgreSQL role separation that `docs/adr/ADR-016-Tenant-Isolation-Model.md` Decision 10 and `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §4.1 already require of **every** relation, so that least-privilege grants exist to be granted **before** any story creates a relation that depends on them. PF-074 creates roles, ownership, grants, revocations, and default privileges, and provisions and verifies them in local development and CI. It creates **no relation, no policy, no schema, and no production credential.**

**Owner.** Platform Foundation. The roles govern the whole database and are platform infrastructure — not one module's concern, and explicitly outside `PlatformAdministration`'s three permitted concepts (Constitution Article 45; `docs/adr/ADR-020-Migration-Rollback-and-Schema-Evolution.md` Decision 11).

**Why it is needed now.** The repository presently has **no role separation at all**, and the required `Application Tests` job implements precisely the configuration §4.1 prohibits:

- `compose.yaml` defines one role, `onelegalpro`, which is the `postgres` service's `POSTGRES_USER` — therefore the **cluster superuser** — and which is simultaneously the `DB_USERNAME` of both the `app` and `queue` services. The local application runtime is a superuser that owns its own database.
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` creates one role, `onelegalpro_test`, which **owns the database**, **runs the migrations**, and **serves the entire suite**. `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §4.1: *"A single database account used for migrations and for serving requests is prohibited."* §15.7 and §18: *"A migration never runs as the application runtime role."*
- `config/database.php` is the stock Laravel skeleton — one `pgsql` connection, no migration or outbox connection, `'search_path'` hardcoded to `'public'`.
- The repository contains **no** `GRANT`, `REVOKE`, `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES`, `CREATE ROLE` outside that one CI line, and **no** tracked `.sql` file.

`PF-033` correctly excluded superuser and `BYPASSRLS` but deliberately **not** ownership, and `CONTRIBUTING.md` already records that limitation in writing: *"the CI/local test login owns its disposable database purely so it can migrate the schema … this job-local convenience is deliberately not that separation."* `PF-073` Decision 11 likewise leaves relation ownership and grants to their owning story. **PF-074 is that story.** Separately, `PA-007`'s Firm registry is an ADR-016 class-(b) relation that deliberately carries **no** Row-Level Security policy, so dedicated privileges are its **only** database-level control; without PF-074 that control cannot be delivered, which is why `PA-007`'s Definition of Ready is unmet.

**Dependencies.** `PF-033` PostgreSQL Continuous Integration — **Done**; its `REQUIRE_POSTGRESQL_TEST_DATABASE` gate is reused **unchanged** and is the mechanism PF-074's feature suite arms itself with. `PF-073` Transaction Manager — **Done**; its two suites must continue to pass byte-identical. Approved architecture: `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §2.4, §2.5, §3.3, §4.1–§4.4, §5.2, §12.2, §12.4, §13.6, §15.5, §15.7, §16.2, §16.3, §18, §23; `docs/architecture/20_Deployment_Operations_Architecture.md` §2.2, §4, §5, §6.1–§6.2, §10.2, §12, §14, §18; `docs/architecture/04_Security_Architecture.md` §1, §5; ADR-016 Decisions 10 and 15; ADR-018 Decisions 2 and 8; ADR-019 Decision 9; ADR-020 Decisions 5, 8, 11; ADR-023 Decisions 6, 12, 13; ADR-024 Decisions 3 and 4. **PF-074 has no code dependency on `PF-064`, on `PA-007`, on `PF-060`–`PF-063`, on `PF-081`/`PF-082`, or on any IdentityAccess story.**

**Approved architecture reconciliation.** Three roles are already accepted architecture, stated twice in identical terms (`03_Database_Design.md` §4.1; `20_Deployment_Operations_Architecture.md` §6.2 — *"exactly as accepted"*; ADR-016 Decision 10; ADR-024 Decision 3). **No contradiction was found, and no architecture document or ADR is amended by this contract.** Two wording points are reconciled here rather than escalated:

1. `20_Deployment_Operations_Architecture.md` §14 prohibits sharing a credential *"between the runtime, migration, and **ownership** database roles"*, while §4.1 and §6.2 both state that the migration role **owns the relations**. This contract reads §14 as three-way credential-separation phrasing, **not** the introduction of a fourth role. **The migration role is the owner.** No separate ownership role is created.
2. `20_Deployment_Operations_Architecture.md` §6.2 scopes the outbox publication role *"(when `PF-091`/`PF-092` introduce it)"*, and no outbox relation exists. Constitution Article 47 permits a release to deliver a subset of approved architecture but **never a weakened control**. Creating the role now **with zero object privileges** is a subset, not a weakening: it delivers the accepted three-role shape, makes the role's isolation from every future relation provable today, and leaves `PF-091`/`PF-092` a pure grant-on-the-outbox-relation change rather than a role-creation change that would re-open the security/authorization approval gate. **The technical owner decided on 14 August 2026 that PF-074 creates the outbox publication role now.**
3. `03_Database_Design.md` §15.5 requires roles and grants to change only through migrations. PF-074's tracked, reviewed `docker/postgres/provision-roles.sh` is therefore the **platform bootstrap migration** for cluster/database roles and database/schema ownership. It runs before Laravel can connect as the migration role because it creates that role and transfers ownership to it. It is **not** a Laravel migration, not a module migration, and not an untracked administrative command. Later relation-specific grants remain in the reviewed Laravel migration that creates the relation. This sequencing satisfies §15.5's recorded-change and review requirement without pretending a role can create itself.

**Technical decisions.**

1. **Exactly three roles, and no fourth.** Their logical definitions are `03_Database_Design.md` §4.1's; their concrete names are PF-074's, since §4.1 line 208 is explicit that it defines *"logical roles and privilege sets, not products, providers, or account names"*. Local development and CI use **distinct** names and **distinct** credentials, because `20_Deployment_Operations_Architecture.md` §3.1 prohibits sharing any credential between two environments, *"including between local development and CI/test"*.

   | Logical role | Local name | CI name |
   |---|---|---|
   | Migration role (and relation owner) | `onelegalpro_migration` | `onelegalpro_test_migration` |
   | Application runtime role | `onelegalpro_app` | `onelegalpro_test_app` |
   | Outbox publication role | `onelegalpro_outbox` | `onelegalpro_test_outbox` |

   Every one of the six is created `LOGIN NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS NOCREATEDB NOCREATEROLE NOREPLICATION INHERIT`. **No role is a member of any other role**, so the runtime role cannot inherit the migration role's authority. **No reporting role, no analytics role, no cross-Firm read role, and no owner exemption is created** — a cross-Firm reporting role does not exist because no Reporting bounded context is approved (Constitution Article 44; `03_Database_Design.md` §4.1 *"Does not exist"*). The pre-existing bootstrap superuser is unchanged and unrenamed — `onelegalpro` (the local `POSTGRES_USER`) and `onelegalpro_bootstrap` (the CI service account) — and **is never a Laravel credential**.

2. **All three roles are `LOGIN`, and there is no `NOLOGIN` group role.** Each must be connected as for its posture to be provable from PostgreSQL's own catalogues and from real permission failures; a `NOLOGIN` outbox role could not be proved unable to read anything. Credentials terminate in exactly two places, both non-secret and both outside production: `.env.example` plus `compose.yaml` for local development (`*_dev_only` values, on an internal Docker network that publishes no port), and the `Application Tests` job's environment block for CI (`*_test_only` values, against an ephemeral service container). **No GitHub Actions secret is introduced, and no secret store, provider, or production credential is selected.**

3. **The migration role owns the database, the schema, and every relation.** Provisioning issues `ALTER DATABASE <database> OWNER TO <migration>` and `ALTER SCHEMA public OWNER TO <migration>`. Because every migration runs as that role, every table, sequence, view, and function it creates is owned by it, including Laravel's own `migrations` repository table. **The application runtime role and the outbox publication role own nothing** — no database, no schema, and no relation — satisfying `03_Database_Design.md` §3.3 item 5 and §16.3, and closing the gap `PF-033` explicitly left open.

4. **Unsafe `PUBLIC` privileges are revoked, and access is granted back explicitly.** PostgreSQL grants `CONNECT` and `TEMPORARY` on a new database to `PUBLIC`, `USAGE` on schema `public` to `PUBLIC`, `EXECUTE` on new functions to `PUBLIC`, and `USAGE` on new types to `PUBLIC`. Provisioning revokes all four and grants back only what a named role needs:

   ```sql
   REVOKE ALL ON DATABASE <database> FROM PUBLIC;
   GRANT CONNECT, TEMPORARY ON DATABASE <database> TO <migration>, <app>;
   GRANT CONNECT              ON DATABASE <database> TO <outbox>;

   REVOKE ALL          ON SCHEMA public FROM PUBLIC;
   GRANT USAGE, CREATE ON SCHEMA public TO <migration>;
   GRANT USAGE         ON SCHEMA public TO <app>, <outbox>;

   ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE <migration> IN SCHEMA public REVOKE ALL     ON TABLES    FROM PUBLIC;
   ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE <migration> IN SCHEMA public REVOKE ALL     ON SEQUENCES FROM PUBLIC;
   ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE <migration> IN SCHEMA public REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTIONS  FROM PUBLIC;
   ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE <migration> IN SCHEMA public REVOKE USAGE   ON TYPES      FROM PUBLIC;
   ```

   **The runtime and outbox roles hold `USAGE` on schema `public` and never `CREATE`.** PostgreSQL 15 already removed `CREATE` on `public` from `PUBLIC`; revoking `ALL` also removes the `USAGE` that remained, so schema access becomes explicit rather than inherited.
