**Every guard-replacement file gained an assertion and lost none**, and no test was lost anywhere.

**Allowlist verification.** Exactly the ten originally allowlisted paths, the five owner-authorized Foundation test files, the three tracking files, and — under the 13 August 2026 correction authorization — `composer.json` and `composer.lock` changed. **Twenty paths in total, and nothing else.** **No production Foundation, Infrastructure, HTTP, model, or provider file is modified** — `git diff` against `d038f4c` over `app/Foundation`, `app/Infrastructure`, `app/Http`, `app/Models`, `app/Providers`, `.github/`, `.githooks/`, `phpunit.xml`, `phpstan.neon.dist`, `pint.json`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `Dockerfile`, `compose.yaml`, `docker/`, `docs/architecture`, `docs/adr`, `docs/domain`, `tests/Feature`, and `tests/Unit/Infrastructure` is **empty**. The four required `Protect main` check names remain exactly `PHP Code Quality`, `Frontend Build`, `Application Tests`, and `Dependency Audit`. The existing file-local fixtures `TestFirmIdentifier` (twice), `TestActorIdentifier`, and `PostgreSqlTestFirmIdentifier` are **unmodified**.

**Forbidden-reference scan.** Across all six module PHP sources, with comments and docblocks stripped, **no** reference exists to `Illuminate`, `Laravel`, `Livewire`, `Inertia`, `Orchestra`, `Facade`, `Carbon`, `Eloquent`, a repository, a service provider, a migration, a schema, `FirmContext`, `App\Application\Tenancy`, `FirmTransactionManager`, the `onelegalpro.firm_id` literal, `CandidateFirmDirectory`, `CandidateFirmReference`, `FirmProvisioning`, `SubscriptionEntitlement`, `EthicalWall`, `PrivilegedAccessGrant`, or `FirmMembership`, and no Laravel global helper is called.

### Implementation decisions the contract left undetermined

Three points were underdetermined and were fixed by explicit owner answer on 12 August 2026, recorded here because each becomes part of a published shape:

1. **`FirmLifecycleState` is placed at `Domain/ValueObjects/FirmLifecycleState.php`**, per *Allowed implementation files* and acceptance criterion 1, which name only the `ValueObjects`, `Aggregates`, and `Events` sub-namespaces. `docs/architecture/19_Platform_Administration_Architecture.md` §28 declares no `Enums/` directory, and none was created.
2. **`FirmName` is bounded at 1 to 200 code points inclusive.** The contract states the rule ("bounds length by code points", empty rejected) but no number, unlike `FirmJurisdiction`'s owner-selected 2–32. The minimum follows from the empty-result rejection; **the maximum was selected by the technical owner** rather than by an implementation author, on the contract's own reasoning that widening is additive and narrowing is a breaking change to a published type.
3. **Unicode NFC normalization uses the global `\Normalizer` class — now backed by an explicitly declared direct dependency.** *(Superseded 13 August 2026; the original wording is retained here as history.)* At implementation, `ext-intl` was installed in neither the canonical Docker image nor either CI job, so `Normalizer` resolved through `symfony/polyfill-intl-normalizer`, a **transitive** package in `composer.lock`, and declaring it directly would have required editing `composer.json`, which acceptance criterion 18 forbade. Independent review found that this **failed the `AGENTS.md` new-runtime-dependency approval gate** rather than merely carrying risk. **Under the 13 August 2026 owner authorization, `symfony/polyfill-intl-normalizer` `^1.38` is now a direct production requirement in `composer.json`.** `ext-intl` remains uninstalled and unclaimed; the declared polyfill is the approved runtime implementation.

### Residual risks, each outside `PA-001`'s scope

- ~~**NFC normalization depends on an undeclared transitive package.**~~ **Resolved 13 August 2026** — see *Resolved review findings* above. `symfony/polyfill-intl-normalizer` `^1.38` is declared directly under `require`, so the package can no longer be dropped by an unrelated dependency change without a visible, reviewed `composer.json` edit. The historical risk is recorded here rather than erased.
- **`FirmName` rejects Unicode control characters (`\p{Cc}`) only.** Unicode **format** characters (`\p{Cf}` — zero-width joiners, bidi overrides, and a leading byte-order mark, which makes two visually identical names unequal) remain acceptable, because the contract says "control character" and widening it here would be an unapproved narrowing of accepted input. A homograph and spoofing policy for Firm names belongs to a later approved story.
- **The 1–200 code-point bound is newly selected, not contract-derived.** Widening it later is additive; narrowing it would be a breaking change requiring explicit human approval.
- **The `PlatformAdministration` guard scanner is duplicated across the five Foundation test files.** The 13 August 2026 authorization for those files permits only the one replaced method in each, so a shared helper would need a new file outside every allowlist. The duplication is deliberate and currently harmless — all five were mutation-tested and behave identically — but de-duplication belongs to its own approved follow-up.

**No claim is made** that the Firm registry, its access posture, Row-Level Security, tenant isolation, `FirmProvisioning`, `SubscriptionEntitlement`, entitlement or seat-limit enforcement, administrative audit persistence, actor attribution, `PF-081` tenant resolution, `PF-082` middleware, IdentityAccess, or any other business module exists — or that anything is deployed or production-ready.

**Objective.** Introduce the minimum `PlatformAdministration`-owned **`Firm` aggregate foundation**: the aggregate root, its owned identifier, its canonical name, its jurisdiction reference, its lifecycle state, and the single recorded creation fact — and nothing else. It is the first dependency in `PF-081`'s blocker **B1**, and it clears only the aggregate-and-identifier half of it.

**Owner.** `PlatformAdministration` (`docs/architecture/19_Platform_Administration_Architecture.md`, `docs/adr/ADR-013-Firm-Provisioning-and-Subscription-Entitlement-Ownership.md`, Constitution Articles 45–48). `PlatformAdministration` **authenticates nothing, authorizes nothing, resolves no membership, constructs no `FirmContext`, inspects no Firm business data, and grants no operator access.**

### Ownership boundaries preserved, not moved

| Concern | Owner | `PA-001` |
|---|---|---|
| `Firm`, `FirmProvisioning`, `SubscriptionEntitlement` | **`PlatformAdministration`** — and nothing else | Delivers the minimum `Firm` foundation only |
| Principals, memberships, authentication, sessions, credentials, invitations, recovery, roles, permissions, privileged access, security events | **IdentityAccess** | **No.** None is created, referenced, stubbed, or approximated |
| `FirmContext`, tenant resolution, tenant middleware | **Platform Foundation** (`PF-080`/`PF-081`/`PF-082`) | **No.** Foundation owns `FirmContext`, never `Firm`; `PA-001` constructs no context |
| Transport-neutral tenant-resolver orchestration and its declared ports | **`PF-081`** | **No.** `PA-001` references no `PF-081` type and proposes no change to its contract |
| HTTP and middleware adaptation | **`PF-082`** | **No.** |
| `Client`, `Matter`, `MatterClient`, `MatterTeam`, `Task`, Ethical Walls | **Practice Management** | **No.** No Firm business data of any kind |
| Invoices, payments, ledgers, client money, `Money`, `Currency` | **Billing**, over Foundation `PF-045` | **No.** No monetary value, and `PF-045` stays `Backlog` |
| `BrandProfile`, `TenantDomain`, theme tokens | **Branding** | **No.** |
| `Jurisdiction` as an authoritative record — its identifier contract, name, official languages, scope, and policy | **Legal Intelligence** (EPIC-002 stage 1, `Backlog`) | **No.** `PA-001` carries an **opaque** reference, resolves nothing, seeds nothing, and defines no jurisdiction identifier contract on Legal Intelligence's behalf |

### Technical decisions

**Approval state, stated precisely.** On 12 August 2026 the technical owner explicitly approved **three** things, and only these three:

- the **`PA-001` identifier and the `PA-` prefix convention** (decision 1);
- the **domain-only scope of `PA-001`** (decision 3);
- the **deferral of the Firm registry relation and the concrete `CandidateFirmDirectory` adapter to `PA-007`** (decision 3).

The owner additionally **selected the opaque jurisdiction-code shape parameters** in decision 7, because fixing them silently would have created an unsupported permanent contract.

**The complete contract has since been accepted (12 August 2026).** The remaining decisions — namespace and file placement (2), the lifecycle scope and the deliberate absence of transitions (4, 5), the absence of an aggregate version (6), and the domain-event and audit-claim boundary (8) — together with the registry-boundary rules, security and audit rules, acceptance criteria, allowed files, exclusions, and test obligations, were this contract's proposal at drafting and are now accepted with it. The three-decision list above records what carried explicit owner approval **at drafting time**, not the current approval state.

**Acceptance did not repair the contract.** Implementation established that three of its own requirements cannot hold at once — see *Blocker* at the head of this entry — and that defect is resolvable only by an owner decision, not by an implementation choice.

**Two points remained undetermined by the accepted contract and were fixed by explicit owner answer**, because leaving either to an implementation author would have created an unsupported permanent contract: `FirmLifecycleState`'s placement in `ValueObjects/`, and `FirmName`'s code-point bounds. Both are recorded under *Implementation decisions the contract left undetermined* at the head of this entry.

1. **Identifier `PA-001`, under the new `PA-` prefix.** No EPIC-012 story previously carried an identifier. The `PA-` prefix names `PlatformAdministration` implementation stories on the convention `PF-` already establishes for Platform Foundation and `ARCH-` for the architecture track, and it is deliberately **not** a `PF-` number, because `PF-` denotes Platform Foundation and would misattribute the owning context.
2. **Namespace `App\Modules\PlatformAdministration\Domain`, at `app/Modules/PlatformAdministration/Domain/`.** This is the first code under `app/Modules`, exactly as `docs/domain/06_Laravel_Module_Blueprint.md` requires for a business capability. The existing `App\` → `app/` PSR-4 mapping already covers it, so **no autoload, namespace, or PSR-4 change is required** — the same reconciliation `PF-073` established for `App\Infrastructure` and `PF-081` for `App\Application`. *(As drafted this read "no `composer.json` change is required". That remained true of **placement**, and is still true of it; the 13 August 2026 correction added one unrelated direct runtime requirement for Unicode normalization, recorded under* Resolved review findings *above.)* **No concrete business identifier is placed in `app/Foundation`**, per the standing Foundation rule.
3. **Domain only. No persistence, and therefore no registry relation.** `PA-001` creates no schema, migration, Eloquent record, repository contract, query contract, adapter, service provider, container binding, route, or controller. The Firm registry relation, its access posture, its narrow read, and the `CandidateFirmDirectory` adapter are **`PA-007`**. This split is deliberate: the class-(b) registry needs module migrations, relation-class declaration, dedicated PostgreSQL privileges, and the ADR-016 Decision 10 role separation, **none of which exists in this repository**, and bundling them here would put a database-design and security change inside a story whose approved scope is the aggregate foundation.
4. **Creation into `Requested` only, with the complete approved state vocabulary declared.** `FirmLifecycleState` declares exactly the four approved cases `Requested`, `Provisioned`, `Active`, and `Closed` (`docs/architecture/19_Platform_Administration_Architecture.md` §7), so `PA-002` cannot invent a different vocabulary and does not have to modify a published type. **Only `Requested` is reachable, and no transition method of any kind exists.** This is absence, not a stub: nothing is renamed, simulated, defaulted, or partially implemented, and **"a Firm never becomes usable as a side effect" is preserved structurally** — no code path can leave `Requested`.
5. **Every `Requested → Provisioned → Active → Closed` transition remains `PA-002`'s**, together with its authorization, its reason recording, and its provisioning semantics. **No correction command is delivered here.** The standing rule is nevertheless fixed by this contract and binds every later story: **a correction is a new recorded transition or fact, never a mutation of history**, on the discipline `docs/adr/ADR-018-Audit-Persistence-and-Append-Only-Enforcement.md` Decision 6 and Constitution Articles 8, 18, 23, and 30 establish.
6. **No aggregate version, and that is not an omission.** `PF-040` records that optimistic-concurrency versioning is a **persistence** concern owned outside Foundation; `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §7.4 requires it where an aggregate's invariants span a request. Because `PA-001` delivers no persistence, the version column and its conflict semantics belong to **`PA-007`**, which must decide them explicitly rather than leaving them to a migration author.
7. **The jurisdiction reference is an opaque reference, not a resolved record and not a country code.** `Jurisdiction` — its identifier contract, code, name, official languages, scope, and `PlatformDefaultJurisdictionPolicy` — is Legal Intelligence's (EPIC-002 stage 1, `Backlog`, unscheduled), and Constitution Article 1 makes jurisdiction a first-class domain concept rather than a hardcoded assumption. `PA-001` carries an **identifier-only cross-bounded-context reference** under `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §8.2, which explicitly accepts that such a reference may be unresolvable and which forbids a foreign key across contexts. It therefore validates a **deliberately minimal canonical shape and nothing else**: enough to reject a malformed value, never enough to assign meaning.

   **A jurisdiction is not synonymous with a country, and `PA-001` must not silently foreclose one that is not.** No approved source declares that a jurisdiction identifier is permanently an ISO alpha-2 country code, so the value object is **not** a country-code type and is **not** constrained to two characters. Future national, subnational, court-system, regulatory, or other separately approved jurisdiction identifiers must remain expressible. `TH` — the seeded Thailand default in `docs/architecture/09_Legal_Intelligence_Architecture.md` — **is a valid example of the shape, never the universal shape.**

   **`PA-001` defines no jurisdiction identifier contract on Legal Intelligence's behalf.** It resolves nothing, seeds no jurisdiction, holds no allow-list, hardcodes no Thailand default, parses no component, and asserts no legal, tax, or regulatory conclusion. When EPIC-002 delivers its own `Jurisdiction`, the authoritative contract is **its** decision; `PA-001`'s opaque reference is deliberately shaped so that adopting it is a mapping exercise rather than a redesign.

   **On the published shape's bounds.** The character set and length bounds below carry **no architectural authority and no semantics** — they exist solely to reject a malformed value. **Widening them later is additive** (no previously accepted value becomes invalid) and is anticipated; **narrowing them would be a breaking change to a published type** requiring explicit human approval. The technical owner selected them on 12 August 2026 for exactly that reason, rather than leaving them to an implementation author.
8. **`PA-001` records one domain event and persists, publishes, dispatches, and logs nothing.** `Firm` extends the `PF-040` `AggregateRoot` and records `FirmCreated` into the in-memory buffer. **No claim is made that administrative audit persistence exists** — that requires `PA-006`, the append-only administrative audit relation, and `PF-091` with its own determined prerequisites, none of which exists.

### Exact scope and deliverable

Six framework-independent domain source files, one module README, and three focused pure-unit suites.

- **`FirmId`** — a `final readonly` **empty marker subclass** of `App\Foundation\Domain\Identity\BusinessIdentifier`, adding **no state, invariant, constructor parameter, factory alias, or behaviour**, per the standing `PF-044` subclass rule recorded in `app/Foundation/README.md`. It is a UUIDv7 with a type, obtained through the existing foundation contract — `FirmId::generate(UuidV7Generator $generator)` or `FirmId::fromString()` — with validation inherited whole from `UuidV7` and never extended. It is **the** Firm identity every other context means, and it **remains distinct from `FirmContext`'s use of the identifier**: `PF-080` declares its Firm parameter as the abstract `BusinessIdentifier`, and **narrowing that declaration to `FirmId` is a separate breaking-contract follow-up requiring explicit human approval, which this story neither performs, requests, nor pre-approves.**
- **`FirmName`** — the canonical name value object. `final readonly`, implementing `ValueObject`, created through a named constructor that trims, rejects any Unicode control character, applies Unicode NFC normalization, rejects an empty result, and bounds length by code points — so a Thai-script name is treated correctly by construction. **It carries no uniqueness of any kind, and none may ever be added** (see *Registry boundary*, below). Collation, sorting, locale-aware comparison, and rendering remain EPIC-012 story 28's.
- **`FirmJurisdiction`** — the **opaque** jurisdiction reference value object. `final readonly`, implementing `ValueObject`, holding one canonical opaque code. **It is not a country-code type**, and its shape is the minimum needed to reject a malformed value:
  - **character set** — uppercase ASCII letters `A`–`Z`, ASCII digits `0`–`9`, and the ASCII hyphen `-` as a **non-semantic separator**;
  - **length** — at least 2 and at most 32 characters;
  - **structure** — no leading hyphen, no trailing hyphen, and no consecutive hyphens; no whitespace, no other punctuation, no non-ASCII character;
  - **canonicalization** — input is trimmed and uppercased **on the creation path only, never during comparison**, so equality stays transitive;
  - **rejection** — anything else is refused through the `PF-049` taxonomy, with the rejected value absent from every message.

  **No seeded list, no country table, no allow-list, no registry lookup, and no Thailand special case.** **The code is never parsed**: no component, prefix, suffix, or hyphen-delimited segment is extracted, interpreted, or exposed, and nothing derives a country, region, court, language, tax treatment, or regulatory meaning from it. `TH` satisfies this shape and is a **valid example, not the universal shape**; `TH-BKK` and a future court-system or regulatory code satisfy it equally, and nothing in the type prefers one form. **Equality compares the canonical opaque value only** and resolves no Legal Intelligence record. It proves nothing about a `Jurisdiction` existing, and holding one grants nothing.
- **`FirmLifecycleState`** — a pure PHP enum with exactly the four approved cases, and no transition method, guard, map, or `is*` helper beyond what the aggregate needs to expose its own state.
- **`Firm`** — `final class Firm extends AggregateRoot`, annotated `@extends AggregateRoot<FirmId>`, holding exactly `FirmName`, `FirmJurisdiction`, and `FirmLifecycleState` beyond its inherited identity. Exactly one named creation constructor produces a `Requested` Firm and records exactly one `FirmCreated` through `recordThat()`. Accessors are read-only. **No setter, no mutation, no transition, no rename, no version, no persistence hook, no serialization, no `__toString`, and no authorization method.**
- **`FirmCreated`** — `final readonly class` implementing exactly `App\Foundation\Domain\Event\DomainEvent`. `eventId(): UuidV7` and `occurredAt(): \DateTimeImmutable` are **supplied as constructor data, never self-generated**: the aggregate reads no ambient time and holds no `Clock` or `UuidV7Generator`, per `PF-043`'s standing convention. `occurredAt()` returns a **plain native `\DateTimeImmutable` in UTC**, never `Carbon\CarbonImmutable`. The payload carries `FirmId`, `FirmName`, `FirmJurisdiction`, and the resulting lifecycle state — identifiers and immutable values only, **never an `Entity` or aggregate reference**.
- **`app/Modules/PlatformAdministration/README.md`** — the module's standing convention record, on the pattern `app/Foundation/README.md` set: what `PlatformAdministration` owns (exactly three concepts), what it may never contain, what `PA-001` delivered, and what remains `PA-002`–`PA-007`.

**No `ModuleServiceProvider.php`, and no `Application/`, `Infrastructure/`, `Interface/`, `Database/`, `Routes/`, `Config/`, or in-module `Tests/` directory is created.** `PF-060` Module Loader, `PF-061` Module Service Provider, and `PF-063` Module Registration are `Backlog`; `PA-001` registers nothing, binds nothing, routes nothing, and migrates nothing, so creating any of them would pre-create another story's type — the same rule `app/Foundation/README.md` states and every Foundation story has followed. The `docs/domain/06_Laravel_Module_Blueprint.md` structure is the module template, not an instruction to create empty directories.

### Firm registry boundary — normative here, implemented by `PA-007`

`PA-001` **defines** this boundary and **implements none of it**. Every rule below binds `PA-007` and is recorded here so the follow-on story inherits it rather than re-deriving it.

- **The Firm registry is the sole ADR-016 Decision 4 class-(b) relation, and no second relation ever enters that class.** One row per Firm; **no `firm_id` column**, because a Firm's identity is its primary key, not a tenancy column; **identity, canonical name, jurisdiction reference, and lifecycle metadata only**; **no Firm business data of any kind, and no field from which any could be reconstructed**; **never a cross-Firm bridge**.
- **Authoritative lookup semantics.** The registry is the single authoritative answer to "does this Firm exist, and what is its lifecycle state" — nothing else may answer it, and no other relation, cache, projection, index, or view duplicates it.
- **It is a pre-`FirmContext` boundary, and is therefore outside ordinary Firm-scoped Row-Level Security.** It cannot use a `FirmContext`-comparing policy, because it is the relation consulted *in order to establish* `FirmContext` (ADR-016 Decision 4a, `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §2.5). **A permissive `USING (true)` policy is prohibited outright as security theatre** — it would present as a control while permitting everything, and would make a genuinely restricted relation indistinguishable from an unrestricted one in any audit of policies.
- **The compensating controls are three, and all three are mandatory.** Dedicated database privileges, with **the Firm-scoped application runtime role holding no general enumeration access**; **narrow named query contracts** returning only the minimized field set a specific purpose needs, never a general "list Firms" query reachable by an arbitrary caller; and **application authorization composed first**, exactly as for any other protected read (Constitution Article 28). **No `BYPASSRLS`, superuser attribute, owner exemption, or role-based permissive policy is introduced anywhere to make registry access work.**
- **Which lookup inputs may identify only a candidate Firm.** A hostname, custom domain, route value, request parameter, header, cookie, request body, or submitted email domain identifies a **candidate** and nothing more. Such a value is never stored as `firm_id`, never treated as an identifier, and never accepted as proof.
- **Registry readability is never membership, and a candidate Firm reference never grants access.** Learning that a Firm exists grants nothing inside it. `FirmContext` is still constructed only from verified identity and active verified membership (Constitution Article 27), which IdentityAccess alone establishes. **Candidate discovery and membership verification remain separate facts**, separated by type and by construction rather than by convention — the structural separation `PF-081` decision 3 already fixes on its own side.
- **Enumeration-resistant failure, and no existence disclosure.** A lookup that finds nothing, a lookup the caller is not authorized to make, and a lookup that cannot be authoritatively completed produce **one externally indistinguishable outcome**. No count, list, ranking, aggregate, search hit, timing signal, or error text distinguishes them, and no failure discloses whether any Firm exists.
- **No cross-Firm list, search, export, or report**, in this context, ever — unless a separately approved decision explicitly requires and authorizes one (Constitution Articles 45 and 48). **No `PlatformAdministration` record, query, cache, projection, index, or event spans two Firms.**
- **No public repository or Eloquent exposure.** No other module imports a `PlatformAdministration` Eloquent record, and `PlatformAdministration` imports none of theirs; cross-module access is published contracts only.
- **No stale-positive semantics and no caching.** A registry answer is read afresh; **no cache, memo, static registry, or singleton state is contracted here**, and introducing one would require its own approved decision defining invalidation and version awareness.
- **Uniqueness on canonical Firm name is prohibited, permanently.** Referential-integrity and unique-constraint checks run **with row security suspended** (`docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §8.5), so a global unique constraint on a business attribute returns a violation to a caller who cannot see the conflicting row — which, for a law firm's name, is an existence disclosure across a conflicts boundary (ADR-016 Decision 12). The defence is the constraint's absence, not the caller's visibility. **The platform-generated-identifier exception covers `id` alone and never extends to a business attribute.**

### `PF-081` adapter boundary

`PF-081` declares `CandidateFirmDirectory` as a **port only** and must not supply an adapter. `PlatformAdministration` owns the registry, its narrow query, and the adapter that satisfies that port.

**The adapter is deferred to `PA-007`, deliberately.** It cannot be written here: it would have to implement `PF-081`'s port, and **no `PF-081` production file exists** — `PF-081` is `Backlog` with its Definition of Ready unmet, and its own approved sequencing decision holds its implementation. Writing a repository or query interface here with no implementer and no consumer would reproduce exactly the unusable, security-shaped abstraction that decision exists to prevent. **`PA-001` therefore declares no repository contract, no query contract, and no port of its own.**

**`PF-081`'s approved contract is unchanged.** No contradiction was found in preparing this contract, and none of `PF-081`'s decisions, API sketch, acceptance criteria, allowed files, exclusions, test plan, or status is altered. The dependency is now explicit and named: **`PA-001` → `PA-007` → IdentityAccess EPIC-009 stages 1 and 2 → reassess `PF-081`.**

**Superseded by `PA-007`'s own contract in one respect.** `PA-001` deferred the adapter to `PA-007`; `PA-007`'s contract establishes that **the adapter is not `PA-007`'s either** — an adapter cannot precede the port it implements. It is **`PA-008`**, sequenced **after** `PF-081` publishes the port. The technical owner resolved the resulting blocker-ordering question on 13 August 2026: **B1, B2, and B3 gate `PF-081`'s implementation start; B4 gates its operational completeness and integration.** `PF-081` acceptance criterion 14 is unchanged. The paragraph above records what was true when `PA-001` was written.

### Security, privacy, and audit rules

- **Fail closed.** Every creation invariant that cannot be satisfied refuses; nothing is defaulted, coerced, truncated, or approximated into validity, and no partially constructed `Firm` is ever observable.
- **Enumeration-safe outcomes**, per the registry boundary above. Existence is never disclosed to an unauthenticated or unauthorized caller through text, response shape, or practicable timing.
- **No caller-controlled exception messages.** Failures use the `PF-049` taxonomy — `InvalidArgument` for unacceptable supplied input, `InvariantViolation` for a broken domain guarantee. **No exception message, code, or property contains the rejected input, a Firm identifier, a Firm name, a jurisdiction value, or a candidate value**, and no message is built from input. Messages are developer-facing diagnostics and are never exposed verbatim to an external caller.
- **`PA-001` logs nothing** and emits no metric, telemetry, or trace. No candidate value, credential, token, session secret, recovery material, membership data, or privileged content is logged anywhere, because nothing is logged at all.
- **No Firm attribution before Firm identity is authoritatively established.** An unverified candidate value never becomes `firm_id` or Firm attribution (Constitution Article 27, `docs/adr/ADR-018-…` Decision 5a).
- **Administrative audit facts are `PlatformAdministration`'s; security events are IdentityAccess's.** Neither duplicates, merges with, or reads the other, and `PlatformAdministration` never stores another context's audit records (Constitution Article 45, ARCH-019 §19, ADR-018 Decision 7). Firm-visible support-access history stays IdentityAccess's.
- **Safe metadata only, append-only, correction as a new fact.** Any future administrative audit record carries actor, Firm, event, result, timestamp, correlation, and authorization provenance — never a credential, session secret, recovery material, privileged narrative, Client or Matter content, or cross-Firm information. History is append-only; correction is a new record referencing the corrected one, never a rewrite; and closing a Firm never destroys the audit fact that it existed.
- **Actor attribution only where an authoritative actor exists — and it does not yet.** IdentityAccess's `Principal` and actor reference are `Backlog` (EPIC-009 stage 1), and ADR-018 Decision 10 requires human, system, integration, and AI actors to stay distinguishable. `PA-001` therefore **records no actor on the `Firm` or on `FirmCreated`, and fabricates none** — accepting an abstract identifier and calling it attribution would manufacture a verified fact from an unverified input. Actor attribution is added by the story that supplies an authoritative actor.
- **No claim that audit persistence exists.** `PA-006`, the append-only administrative audit relation, and `PF-091` with its own determined prerequisites do not exist. Recording a domain event in memory is not an audit record, is not durable, and is never described as one.
- **A `FirmId` is not a secret and not a capability.** Possessing one proves nothing and grants nothing; it discloses its own creation time to millisecond precision by construction; and it is never a session token, credential, magic link, recovery code, or proof of identity. It is nevertheless sensitive metadata and is never placed in a URL parameter, query string, or any position routinely logged by intermediaries.
- **`PA-001` is not an authorization or Firm-isolation control.** It composes no authorization, decides no Ethical Wall — `CheckEthicalWallAccess` remains Practice Management's alone, and Release 0.1 has no wall to compose, an absence that is never stubbed — and completes no part of tenant isolation. Application-layer resolution, Firm-scoped repositories, forced Row-Level Security, and executed PostgreSQL isolation tests are the four independent layers ADR-016 Decision 5 requires; **`PA-001` delivers none of them.**
- **This is a business-module and Firm-isolation-adjacent change and therefore hits the `AGENTS.md` approval gate** before implementation, independently of this contract's approval.

### Dependencies

*Done and consumed:*

- **`PF-049` Foundation Exception Hierarchy, `PF-042` ValueObject, `PF-047` Clock, `PF-048` UUIDv7, `PF-044` BusinessIdentifier, `PF-041` Entity, `PF-043` DomainEvent, `PF-040` AggregateRoot — all Done.** `FirmId` extends `BusinessIdentifier`; `Firm` extends `AggregateRoot`; `FirmCreated` implements `DomainEvent`; the value objects implement `ValueObject` and throw through the `PF-049` taxonomy. Each is consumed **unchanged**, and no Foundation source or test is added or edited.

*Done and deliberately **not** consumed:*

- **`PF-080` Firm Context — Done.** `PA-001` constructs no `FirmContext`. The registry is a **pre-context** class-(b) boundary, so the ordering is not circular: the registry legitimately exists before context does. `docs/architecture/19_Platform_Administration_Architecture.md` §28's statement that every stage depends on `PF-080` is satisfied by `PF-080` being Done; `PA-001` imports it nowhere.
- **`PF-073` Transaction Manager — Done.** No transaction is opened, and `App\Infrastructure\Database\FirmTransactionManager` is neither imported nor invoked.
- **`PF-033` PostgreSQL Continuous Integration — Done.** `PA-001` adds no database-backed test.

*Approved architecture:*

- Constitution Articles 5, 26–30, and 45–48; `docs/architecture/19_Platform_Administration_Architecture.md` §§4, 6, 7, 16, 17, 18, 19, 22, 23, 28; `docs/adr/ADR-013-…` Decisions 1, 2, 3, 11, 12, 13; `docs/adr/ADR-016-Tenant-Isolation-Model.md` Decisions 3, 4, 4a, 5, 10, 11, 12; `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §§2.4, 2.5, 5.1, 7.1, 7.4, 8.2, 8.3, 8.5, 9.2; `docs/adr/ADR-018-…` Decisions 5a, 6, 7, 9, 10; `docs/adr/ADR-014-Operator-Assisted-Onboarding-and-Privileged-Access.md`; `docs/adr/ADR-015-Deferred-Professional-Responsibility-Controls.md`.

*Not required, because deliberately out of scope — each named rather than worked around:*

- **`PF-091` Transactional Outbox and its genuine prerequisites**, which remain undetermined by that story's own approved analysis. Required for durable administrative audit (`PA-006`), not for `PA-001`.
- **PostgreSQL role separation, dedicated privileges, and forced Row-Level Security** (ADR-016 Decisions 5, 6, 10). Required for `PA-007`, not for `PA-001`.
- **Idempotency persistence (`docs/adr/ADR-021-Idempotency-Persistence.md`), transactional boundaries, and optimistic-concurrency versioning** — persistence concerns, `PA-007`'s.
- **IdentityAccess EPIC-009 stages 1 and 2**, for actor and authorization provenance on audited transitions. Required by `PA-002` and by any audited transition; `PA-001` delivers no transition and asserts no actor.

*Future dependents, never dependencies:* `PA-002` through `PA-007`; IdentityAccess EPIC-009; `PF-081`; `PF-082`; every Firm-scoped repository, RLS policy, and business module.

**Ordering findings, stated honestly.** **This foundation can be implemented before `PF-081`** — `PF-081` consumes a Firm identifier, never a `Firm` record, and the class-(b) registry exists precisely before `FirmContext`. **`PA-007` can also be implemented before IdentityAccess**, because neither the registry nor its adapter authenticates anyone or resolves membership; what `PA-007` genuinely requires is PostgreSQL role separation and privileges, not identity. **What cannot be done before IdentityAccess is actor attribution**, and what cannot be done before `PF-091` and `PA-006` is durable administrative audit — which is precisely why `PA-001` claims neither.

### Definition of Ready — met (both former counts resolved 12 August 2026)

*Resolved:* objective; owner; ownership boundaries; identifier and prefix convention; namespace and file placement; exact aggregate scope; identifier representation; canonical-name rules; jurisdiction-reference representation; lifecycle scope and the deliberate absence of transitions; registry boundary and its compensating controls; the `PF-081` adapter boundary and its deferral to `PA-007`; security, failure, confidentiality, enumeration-resistance, and audit rules; dependency analysis; acceptance criteria; allowed and forbidden files; explicit exclusions; test obligations; validation plan; sequencing; documentation impact.

*The two counts that formerly blocked readiness, and how each was resolved on 12 August 2026:*

1. **Contract acceptance — resolved.** The contract was accepted by the technical owner. It had previously carried explicit owner approval of three decisions only — the identifier and `PA-` prefix, the domain-only scope, and the deferral of the registry relation and adapter to `PA-007` — plus the owner's selection of the opaque jurisdiction-code shape parameters.
2. **The first-business-module authorization gate — resolved.** The repository owner recorded the explicit authorization quoted at the head of this entry, satisfying the `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` rule *"Do not begin business modules until separately approved."* It is recorded separately from contract acceptance, as that rule requires, and **expands no scope and extends no allowlist.**

**No dependency is missing and no substitute was proposed** — every technical dependency is Done.

**At that point `PA-001` entered `Code Review`; it subsequently completed final confirmation, protected checks, approval, merge, and cleanup and is now `Done`.** The contract defect discovered during implementation — three of its own requirements that could not hold simultaneously — is recorded and resolved under *Resolved contract defect* at the head of this entry, under its own recorded owner authorization.

### Acceptance criteria

A future `PA-001` implementation is correct only if all of the following hold:

1. The six source types exist at exactly `app/Modules/PlatformAdministration/Domain/` in namespace `App\Modules\PlatformAdministration\Domain`, in the sub-namespaces `ValueObjects`, `Aggregates`, and `Events` as listed in *Allowed implementation files*.
2. `FirmId` is `final readonly`, extends `BusinessIdentifier`, and **declares no member of its own** — no property, constant, method, factory alias, or constructor.
3. `FirmId` never equals another `BusinessIdentifier` subtype wrapping the identical UUID, **in both directions**, with no `\TypeError`.
4. `FirmName` and `FirmJurisdiction` are `final readonly`, implement `ValueObject`, canonicalize on the creation path and never during comparison, and are never observable in an invalid state.
4a. `FirmJurisdiction` accepts exactly the published opaque shape — `A`–`Z`, `0`–`9`, and a non-leading, non-trailing, non-consecutive ASCII hyphen; length 2 to 32 inclusive — and **imposes no two-character, ISO, or country-code restriction of any kind**. `TH` is accepted; so are longer and hyphenated codes such as `TH-BKK`.
4b. `FirmJurisdiction` **exposes no parsing, component, segment, prefix, suffix, country, region, court, language, tax, or regulatory accessor**, holds no allow-list, seeded list, country table, or Thailand special case, and performs no lookup. Its equality compares the canonical opaque value only and resolves nothing in Legal Intelligence.
5. `FirmLifecycleState` declares **exactly** the four approved cases and no transition method.
6. `Firm` is `final`, extends `AggregateRoot`, declares `@extends AggregateRoot<FirmId>`, and exposes exactly its identity, name, jurisdiction, and lifecycle-state accessors plus the inherited `releaseEvents()`.
7. **No path can leave `Requested`** — no transition, setter, mutator, rename, `with*`, state-assignment, or reflection-free mutation member exists on `Firm`.
8. Creation records **exactly one** `FirmCreated`; `releaseEvents()` returns it once, in recording order, and leaves the buffer empty.
9. `FirmCreated` is `final readonly`, implements exactly `DomainEvent`, receives its `UuidV7` and `\DateTimeImmutable` as constructor data, returns a **plain native `\DateTimeImmutable` whose timezone is UTC**, and carries no `Entity`, aggregate, mutable object, or confidential value. **"Whose timezone is UTC" means the timezone *identity* is exactly `UTC`** — a zero offset alone does not satisfy it, because a zero offset is a property of an instant rather than of a zone, and `GMT`, `Z`, `Etc/UTC`, and a fixed `+00:00` are distinct identities this contract does not name. An instant that fails the rule is **rejected, never converted**. *(Clarified 13 August 2026 after independent review found the original implementation testing the offset instead; the criterion's meaning is unchanged.)*
10. `Firm` and its value objects read **no ambient time and no ambient randomness**, and hold **no `Clock` and no `UuidV7Generator`**.
11. No exception message, code, or property contains a rejected input value, a Firm identifier, a Firm name, a jurisdiction value, or any candidate value.
12. **No repository, query contract, port, Eloquent model, migration, schema, RLS policy, service provider, container binding, route, controller, middleware, adapter, cache, logger, or `PF-081` reference exists anywhere under `app/Modules`.**
13. No production file under `app/Modules` imports Laravel, Illuminate, Eloquent, HTTP, queue, container, facade, configuration, vendor SDK, or database symbols, and no Laravel global helper is called.
14. **No Foundation or Infrastructure source or test is added or edited**, and `tests/Unit/Foundation/FoundationLayerHasNoFrameworkDependenciesTest.php`, `tests/Unit/Foundation/Tenancy/FirmContextTest.php`, `tests/Unit/Infrastructure/Database/FirmTransactionManagerTest.php`, and `tests/Feature/Infrastructure/Database/FirmTransactionManagerPostgreSqlTest.php` all pass **byte-identical and unchanged**.
15. The existing file-local test fixtures `TestFirmIdentifier` (twice) and `PostgreSqlTestFirmIdentifier` are **left in place and unmodified**. `PA-001` does not migrate them to `FirmId`; doing so would narrow `PF-080`'s abstract parameter types and is a separate breaking-contract follow-up requiring explicit approval.
16. No `FirmProvisioning`, `SubscriptionEntitlement`, entitlement, seat-limit, principal, membership, session, credential, invitation, role, capability, permission, authorization, branding, domain, monetary, operator-access, Ethical Wall, or Firm-business-data type is created — and none is stubbed, approximated, simulated, partially implemented, or renamed.
17. No Firm-level **suspended** state, Firm-wide disable, emergency shutdown, self-service signup path, or provisioning workflow exists in any form.
18. `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `phpunit.xml`, `phpstan.neon.dist`, `pint.json`, `.github/`, `.githooks/`, and Docker/environment files are unchanged. **`composer.json` and `composer.lock` change only as the 13 August 2026 correction authorization permits** — one direct production requirement, `symfony/polyfill-intl-normalizer: ^1.38`, and the resulting `content-hash`. **No other dependency is added, upgraded, downgraded, or removed**, no package moves between `require` and `require-dev`, and no repository, stability, platform, script, or Composer configuration setting changes. *(As originally written this criterion required `composer.json` and `composer.lock` to be unchanged and no dependency to be added; independent review established that this made the `AGENTS.md` new-runtime-dependency gate unsatisfiable, and the owner authorized this narrow supersession.)*
19. The four required `Protect main` check names remain exactly `PHP Code Quality`, `Frontend Build`, `Application Tests`, and `Dependency Audit`.

### Focused test plan

Three pure unit suites under `tests/Unit/Modules/PlatformAdministration/Domain/`, each extending `PHPUnit\Framework\TestCase` directly, **booting no Laravel application and touching no database**. Test-local fixtures — a foreign `BusinessIdentifier` marker subtype, a scripted `UuidV7Generator`, and a fixed `Clock` where an instant is needed — are declared **inside the test files**, per the standing rule that no test double belongs under `app/`.

*`PlatformAdministrationContractShapeTest`* proves by reflection: exact namespaces and file locations; `FirmId` final, readonly, extending `BusinessIdentifier`, declaring no member; `Firm` final, extending `AggregateRoot`, with the exact public member list and the named absence of every setter, transition, version, persistence, serialization, and authorization member; `FirmLifecycleState`'s exact four cases; `FirmCreated` final, readonly, implementing exactly `DomainEvent`; each value object final, readonly, implementing `ValueObject`; that `app/Modules` contains exactly the six approved source files plus the README; and that no file under `app/Modules` references a forbidden framework namespace, a Laravel global helper, `FirmTransactionManager`, the `onelegalpro.firm_id` literal, `FirmContext`, or any `App\Application\Tenancy` type.

*`FirmTest`* proves behaviourally: creation yields `Requested` and preserves the exact supplied identifier, name, and jurisdiction instances; exactly one `FirmCreated` is recorded, carrying the same values; `releaseEvents()` returns it once and a repeated release returns an empty list; two `Firm`s are the same aggregate only when their runtime class and `FirmId` match; and **no reachable member changes the lifecycle state** — asserted against the enumerated public and protected member list, not merely by absence of a named method.

*`FirmValueObjectsTest`* proves behaviourally: `FirmId` generation through a scripted generator invoked exactly once and retained nowhere; round trip through `fromString()`; uppercase and mixed-case canonicalization; cross-type inequality in both directions with no `\TypeError`; `FirmName` trimming, NFC normalization, Thai-script acceptance, code-point length bounds, and a rejection matrix covering empty, whitespace-only, control-character, and over-length input; and, for `FirmJurisdiction`, that it behaves as an **opaque** reference rather than a country code — **acceptance** of `TH`, of a two-character minimum, of a 32-character maximum, of a digit-bearing code, and of hyphenated codes such as `TH-BKK` and a longer multi-segment form, each asserted explicitly so a future reintroduction of a two-character or ISO restriction fails the suite; **canonicalization** of lowercase and mixed-case input and of surrounding whitespace to the same canonical value, applied on creation and never during comparison; **equality** on the canonical opaque value alone, with cross-type comparison returning `false` in both directions and no `\TypeError`; **absence, by name, of every parsing, component, segment, country, region, court, language, tax, and regulatory accessor**, and of any allow-list, seeded list, or lookup; and a **rejection matrix** covering the empty string, whitespace only, a single character, 33 characters, a leading hyphen, a trailing hyphen, consecutive hyphens, a hyphen-only value, internal whitespace, lowercase-only punctuation such as `.` and `_`, and a non-ASCII character. Every rejection path asserts that **no exception message contains its rejected input**, for `FirmId`, `FirmName`, and `FirmJurisdiction` alike.

### Full validation plan

In the canonical Docker PHP 8.4 / PostgreSQL 16 environment, per the documented `CONTRIBUTING.md` recipe: `git diff --check`; `composer validate --strict`; `composer pint:test`; `composer phpstan` at level 5 with no baseline, suppression, or ignored error; the three focused suites; `tests/Unit/Foundation/FoundationLayerHasNoFrameworkDependenciesTest.php`, `tests/Unit/Foundation/Tenancy/FirmContextTest.php`, `tests/Feature/PostgreSqlTestDatabaseGuardTest.php`, and both `FirmTransactionManager` suites passing **byte-identical and unchanged**; the complete PostgreSQL-backed suite passing with a recorded test and assertion count **strictly above the measured pre-implementation baseline**; and `composer audit --locked --abandoned=report` and `npm audit --audit-level=high` both clean. Independent review and architecture review record no unresolved P0, P1, or P2.
