### Forbidden files and changes

No change to **any** existing file under `app/Foundation`, `app/Infrastructure`, `app/Http`, `app/Models`, or `app/Providers`, and **no change to any existing test**. No change to `composer.json`, `composer.lock`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `phpunit.xml`, `phpstan.neon.dist`, `pint.json`, `.github/`, `.githooks/`, Docker or environment files, Laravel bootstrap or configuration, service providers, routes, controllers, middleware, jobs, commands, migrations, schema, RLS policies, PostgreSQL roles/grants/functions, repositories, outbox, audit, or idempotency persistence. **No architecture or ADR file may be edited by the implementation story.** **No required check, test, assertion, guard, static-analysis level, formatting rule, security control, or approval gate may be removed, weakened, renamed, skipped, or suppressed.**

### Explicit exclusions

`PA-001` delivers none of, and must not stub, approximate, simulate, partially implement, or rename any of: `FirmProvisioning` and every `Requested → Provisioned → Active → Closed` transition; a **Firm-level suspended state**, Firm-wide disable, or emergency shutdown of any kind; self-service Firm signup; any provisioning workflow, activation-on-first-login path, or implicit activation; `SubscriptionEntitlement`, entitlement term/status evaluation, lapse semantics, or the published entitlement query; seat limits and seat accounting; the Firm registry relation, its schema, migration, privileges, access posture, repository, query, or adapter; the concrete `CandidateFirmDirectory` adapter; authentication, credentials, password hashing, MFA, passkeys, magic links, invitations, recovery, lockout, sessions, principals, actor categories, service principals, delegation, acting-on-behalf-of, privileged-access or break-glass grants; `FirmMembership` and its lifecycle; authorization, roles, capabilities, permissions, deny rules, or authorization caching; **Ethical Walls of any shape** — no wall, flag, allow-list, restricted-matter marker, or per-actor visibility rule; branding, `BrandProfile`, `TenantDomain`, theme tokens, custom domains; pricing, plans, tiers, invoices, payments, ledgers, balances, tax, `Money`, `Currency` (`PF-045` and `PF-046` remain `Backlog` and deferred); operator access paths of any kind; cross-Firm reporting, analytics, benchmarking, or comparison; every item of Firm business data — `Client`, `Matter`, `MatterClient`, `MatterTeam`, `Task`, documents, communications, financial content; database schema, migrations, RLS policies or functions, roles, grants, connections, transactions, or the transaction-local Firm setting; repositories, Eloquent models, casts, DTOs, persistence, serialization; caches of any kind; events dispatch, outbox, audit persistence, security-event recording, logging, telemetry, or metrics; HTTP middleware, controllers, routes, request or response handling, container bindings, service providers; queues, jobs, workers, schedulers; test doubles under `app/`; and every other business module. **`PA-002` through `PA-007`, `PF-081`, and `PF-082` are not silently implemented, in whole or in part, by `PA-001`.**

### Documentation impact

On implementation: this entry and `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` record verified status and delivery evidence; `docs/implementation/01_Implementation_Sprint_Plan.md` records the lifecycle note; and `app/Modules/PlatformAdministration/README.md` becomes the module's standing convention record. **No architecture or ADR file is edited by implementation.** No architectural contradiction requiring an ADR or Constitution amendment was found in preparing this contract: the composite `PF-081` blocker B1 is reconciled by decision 3's `PA-001`/`PA-007` split without altering `PF-081`'s contract; `docs/architecture/19_Platform_Administration_Architecture.md` §22's conceptual event sketch omits a Firm-creation event while §19 requires "Firm created" as a mandatory administrative audit fact, and §22 is explicitly conceptual, so `FirmCreated` is consistent with ARCH-019 rather than an exception to it; and `app/Foundation/README.md` needs no change, because `PA-001` places no code under `app/Foundation` and edits no Foundation file.

### Definition of Done

`PA-001` is Done only when: this contract has passed independent review and received recorded final technical acceptance; the first-business-module authorization gate has been explicitly satisfied and recorded; the accepted contract is implemented strictly within its allowlist; every acceptance criterion above passes; the three focused suites pass; the unchanged `PF-049` Foundation guard and `PF-033` PostgreSQL engine/role guard pass byte-identical; the complete canonical PostgreSQL-backed suite passes with no removed, weakened, renamed, skipped, or suppressed test, assertion, guard, static-analysis level, formatting rule, security control, or approval gate; Pint, PHPStan level 5 (no baseline, suppression, or ignored error), `composer validate --strict`, both dependency audits, and `git diff --check` pass; independent review reports no unresolved P0, P1, or P2; **all four required protected checks pass on the exact final implementation head**; the required human approval comment is recorded; the PR merges to `main`; tracking records only verified evidence; and the implementation branch and worktree are removed locally and remotely.

**Done would make no claim** that the Firm registry, its access posture, Row-Level Security, tenant isolation as a whole, `FirmProvisioning`, `SubscriptionEntitlement`, entitlement or seat-limit enforcement, administrative audit persistence, actor attribution, `PF-081` tenant resolution, `PF-082` middleware, IdentityAccess, or any other business module exists — or that Release 0.1 is production-ready.

### Sequencing decision and milestone effect

**Sequencing.** `PA-001` → `PA-007` (registry relation, access posture, narrow query, and the `CandidateFirmDirectory` adapter) → IdentityAccess EPIC-009 stage 1 → IdentityAccess EPIC-009 stage 2 → reassess `PF-081` readiness under the `AGENTS.md` gate. `PA-002` through `PA-006` follow their own approved contracts and are not made Ready by this entry.

**Superseded in one respect by `PA-007`'s own contract, recorded below.** `PA-007` no longer carries the `CandidateFirmDirectory` adapter, and it acquired two blocking prerequisites — PostgreSQL role separation and module migration discovery — that this sentence did not name. The current sequence is recorded in `PA-007`'s *Sequencing decision and milestone effect*. The wording above is preserved as the historical record of `PA-001`'s own decision.

**Milestone effect: none.** **31 August 2026** (public website and sales readiness) and **15 September 2026** (planned secure production service start) remain **targets, not contractual commitments** (`docs/architecture/02_Product_Requirements.md` §1 and §8). Recording a contract advances neither, and the outstanding production-access evidence is unchanged: an executed and recorded restore test, operational monitoring, a documented incident procedure, the Thai-qualified legal review required by `docs/adr/ADR-012-…` Decision 8, and every applicable `AGENTS.md` approval gate.

## PA-007 — Firm Registry Relation and Narrow Registry Query — Backlog (Definition of Ready **met**, implementation **not authorized**)

**Status.** `Backlog`. **Not** In Progress, Code Review, Architecture Review, QA, Approved, or Done. **Its Definition of Ready is met as of 17 August 2026**, both blocking dependencies — `PF-074` and `PF-064` — being `Done` and merged to `main`. **Ready is not authorization.** **No `PA-007` implementation has started**, no `PA-007` production file exists, and no relation, migration, database role, grant, privilege, or policy exists anywhere in this repository. Nothing is deployed. This entry is the story **contract** only; recording a contract never schedules or starts a story, and **meeting the Definition of Ready authorizes no implementation, deployment, production access, schema creation, migration execution, role creation, grant, or security change** — `PA-007` is a database-design and security/authorization change under `AGENTS.md` and requires its own separate explicit owner authorization.

**Permanent name, decided by the technical owner on 13 August 2026.** `PA-007` was previously named *Firm Registry Relation and `CandidateFirmDirectory` Adapter*. Technical decision 1 below removes the adapter from its scope, so retaining a title naming a deliverable the story does not deliver would itself be the practice `docs/adr/ADR-012-Release-0-1-Product-Scope-and-Matter-Desk-Slice.md` prohibits — describing an absent control as present. **The retitle is permanent.** The identifier `PA-007` is unchanged, the former name is recorded here so every existing cross-reference remains traceable, and **the adapter is not deleted from the plan of record**: it is `PA-008`.

**Technical-owner ratifications recorded 13 August 2026.** Four architecture-derived decisions in this contract are explicitly ratified and are no longer open: **no `firm_id` foreign key** to the registry (decision 10); **no optimistic-concurrency version column** (decision 4); **no idempotency relation or record** (decision 5); and **`FirmTransactionManager` does not apply** to this pre-`FirmContext` class-(b) relation (decision 9). Each remains reversible only by a new recorded decision, not by a migration author.

**Objective.** Deliver the single ADR-016 Decision 4 class-(b) **Firm registry relation** — the sole relation whose rows *are* Firms — together with the Decision 4a access posture that restricts it without Row-Level Security, and **one narrow, `PlatformAdministration`-owned named query** answering exactly "does this Firm exist, and what is its lifecycle state" for a caller that already holds an exact Firm identifier. `PA-007` authenticates nobody, verifies no membership, constructs no `FirmContext`, authorizes no resource, enumerates no Firms, writes no row, and implements no other context's port.

**Owner.** `PlatformAdministration`. The relation, its migration, its privileges, and its query contract are this context's. The application-layer tenancy port remains `PF-081`'s, and the two ownerships stay distinct.

### Ownership boundaries preserved, not moved

| Concern | Owner | `PA-007` |
|---|---|---|
| The `Firm` aggregate, `FirmId`, `FirmName`, `FirmJurisdiction`, `FirmLifecycleState`, `FirmCreated` | `PA-001` — **Done** | **No.** Consumed unchanged; not modified, extended, or re-shaped. |
| The class-(b) Firm registry relation, its class declaration, privileges, and narrow read | **`PA-007`** | **Yes.** |
| The three platform-wide PostgreSQL roles and the migration/runtime account split | **`PF-074`** — **`Done`**; merged to `main` on 16 August 2026 by pull request #61 | **No.** Was a blocking dependency; now satisfied. |
| Module migration discovery and execution | **`PF-064`** — Platform Foundation; contract recorded under EPIC-001 | **No.** Named as a blocking dependency. |
| `App\Application\Tenancy\CandidateFirmDirectory` and every other tenancy port | `PF-081` — `Backlog` | **No.** Not declared, not implemented, not imported. |
| The concrete adapter satisfying that port | **`PA-008`** — assigned 13 August 2026; scope outline recorded, no full contract, `Backlog` | **No.** See decision 1. |
| Firm creation, lifecycle transitions, and their authorization | `PA-002` — `Backlog`, reserved | **No.** The registry has no writer in `PA-007`. |
| Administrative audit persistence and actor attribution | `PA-006` — `Backlog`, reserved | **No.** See decision 8. |
| Outbox rows, publication, and per-Firm registry iteration | `PF-091`, `PF-092` — `Backlog` | **No.** See decision 7. |
| HTTP extraction, middleware, request adaptation, container binding | `PF-082` — `Backlog` | **No.** |
| `BrandProfile`, `TenantDomain`, custom domains, hostnames | Branding, EPIC-003 — no implementation track | **No.** See decision 6. |
| Firm membership, principals, sessions, authorization decisions | IdentityAccess, EPIC-009 stages 1 and 2 — `Backlog` | **No.** |

### Technical decisions

1. **The concrete `CandidateFirmDirectory` adapter is not `PA-007`'s. It is `PA-008`, sequenced *after* `PF-081` publishes the port.** It cannot be written here: `PF-081` owns `app/Application/Tenancy/CandidateFirmDirectory.php` in **its own** allowed-files list and states it "must not supply the adapter", so the port type does not exist for `PA-007` to implement against, and declaring a substitute port here would create the unusable, security-shaped abstraction `PF-081`'s own sequencing decision exists to prevent. **The technical owner resolved the resulting blocker-ordering question on 13 August 2026**: **B4 is not a prerequisite for implementing `PF-081`'s port and resolver**; it is a prerequisite for an **executable end-to-end candidate-resolution path and final operational integration**. `PF-081` may begin implementation once **B1, B2, and B3** are cleared, and **must not be declared operationally complete or integrated until B4 exists**. `PF-081` acceptance criterion 14 is **unchanged and correct** — `PF-081` itself creates no production implementation of `CandidateFirmDirectory`. **`PA-007` therefore clears the registry half of B1 and does not clear B4**, and no `PF-081` decision, API sketch, acceptance criterion, allowed file, exclusion, test-plan item, security rule, ownership boundary, or status is altered by this entry.

2. **`PA-007` publishes its own narrow query contract, and that contract is not `PF-081`'s port.** `PlatformAdministration` owns the registry and therefore owns the named contract through which the registry is read, exactly as ADR-016 Decision 4a and `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §2.5 require — "narrow repository and query contracts … named contracts that return the specific fields tenant resolution or an authorized `PlatformAdministration` path requires". When `PF-081`'s port later exists, **`PA-008`** writes a thin implementation of that port delegating to this contract. **This is not a duplicated abstraction**: one is `PlatformAdministration`'s published read contract, the other is Platform Foundation's application-layer port, and the two are owned by different contexts by design. **`PA-007` must not widen this contract in anticipation of `PA-008`** — no method, parameter, or returned field is added because an adapter will later exist.

3. **Read-only. The registry has no writer in `PA-007`.** No insert, update, delete, upsert, or lifecycle-transition path is created. Writing the registry is inseparable from provisioning authorization and administrative audit, which are `PA-002` and `PA-006`; delivering a write path here would either fabricate an authorization decision or persist a Firm with no audit fact, and both are prohibited. Test rows are created as **test-local fixtures inside the test files**, never by production code and never by a seeder.

4. **No optimistic-concurrency version column, decided explicitly rather than left to a migration author.** `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §7.4 mandates optimistic concurrency "for aggregates whose invariants span a request". Under decision 3 the registry has no update path at all, `PA-001`'s `Firm` has **no transition method of any kind** with only `Requested` reachable, and `PlatformAdministrationContractShapeTest` forbids the members `version`, `aggregateVersion`, `expectedVersion`, `sequenceNumber`, and `concurrencyToken`. There is therefore no contended update and no invariant spanning a request, and a version column would be speculative persistence for a mutation path that does not exist. **The decision, its conflict semantics, and the column that carries it belong to `PA-002`**, whose first lifecycle-transition write is the moment the concern becomes real. `PA-001`'s decision 6 assigned this question to `PA-007`; `PA-007` answers it, and records where the obligation moves.

5. **ADR-021 idempotency does not apply, and no idempotency theatre is added.** `docs/adr/ADR-021-Idempotency-Persistence.md` Decision 12 scopes Release 0.1 idempotency to "wherever a command may be retried after an ambiguous failure". Under decision 3 there is no command and no retryable mutation. **No idempotency key, record, relation, or scoped-key constraint is created**, and none is implied for a later story by this absence.

6. **The only accepted lookup input is an exact, canonical Firm identifier.** No approved document defines a hostname, custom-domain, email-domain, or any other candidate-key mapping to a Firm. `TenantDomain` is **Branding's** concept (`docs/architecture/19_Platform_Administration_Architecture.md` §14, `docs/architecture/16_Identity_Security_Access_Control_Architecture.md` §29), Branding has no implementation track, and the registry's approved field set is "identity, canonical name, jurisdiction reference, and lifecycle metadata only" — so a hostname or domain column would place Branding's data inside a class-(b) relation, which §2.4 and §2.5 forbid. **`PA-007` therefore invents no `TenantDomain`, no host mapping, no branding or domain-ownership concept, and no alternative candidate key.** A caller supplying a hostname, custom domain, route value, request parameter, header, cookie, request body, or submitted email domain has supplied a **candidate and nothing more**; such a value is never stored, never treated as an identifier, and never accepted as proof. Whether any such mapping should ever exist is a separate approved decision, and this entry neither designs nor pre-authorizes one.

7. **No enumeration query, not even for the outbox.** `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §2.5 forbids "a general 'list Firms' query available to arbitrary callers". `docs/adr/ADR-019-Transactional-Outbox-Persistence.md` Decision 9 does grant the outbox publication role `SELECT` on the registry to iterate Firms — but that consumer is `PF-091`/`PF-092`, both `Backlog`, and defining an iteration contract with no implementer would repeat the mistake decision 1 avoids. `PA-007` grants the outbox publication role the `SELECT` privilege its approved posture requires and **defines no iteration query**; the iteration contract is `PF-091`/`PF-092`'s to define when they exist.

8. **`PA-007` persists no audit record, and the gap is recorded rather than papered over.** §2.5 requires registry enumeration and returned fields be "audited where required", but there is no relation in which a **pre-`FirmContext`** registry read could be audited: `PlatformAdministration`'s administrative audit stream is `PA-006` (does not exist) and is Firm-scoped, therefore unwritable before `FirmContext` exists; and the only non-Firm-scoped audit relation is ADR-016 class (d), which is **IdentityAccess's alone**, is closed by decision, and which Constitution Article 45 forbids `PlatformAdministration` from duplicating. **`PA-007` claims no audit persistence, no actor attribution, and no security-event recording**, and creates no relation, column, log, or event as a substitute for one. This is a recorded architectural gap for `PA-006`, not an omission `PA-007` may quietly fill.

9. **`App\Infrastructure\Database\FirmTransactionManager` does not apply and is neither imported nor invoked.** It exists to establish the transaction-local `onelegalpro.firm_id` setting for **class-(a)** relations inside a verified `FirmContext` (ADR-016 Decision 8). The registry is **class (b)**, read *before* `FirmContext` exists; invoking the manager would require fabricating a `FirmContext` the caller has not earned. `app/Infrastructure/Database/FirmTransactionManager.php` and both its suites remain **byte-identical and unchanged**, and no `PA-007` file contains the literal `onelegalpro.firm_id` or calls `set_config(`. The read opens **no transaction of its own**: §4.4's transaction requirement binds Firm-scoped relations, and the registry is not one.

10. **`firm_id` carries no foreign key to the registry.** This closes the open item `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §22.1 assigns to "the implementing story for `PF-080` / `PlatformAdministration`", which `PF-080` did not decide and which §8.3, ADR-016, and ADR-017 each record as open. `PA-007` creates the referenced side and therefore records the decision: **identifier-only, no foreign key**, ratifying §8.3's stated reasoning — a foreign key would make every module's schema depend on `PlatformAdministration`'s and give one context's referential actions authority over every other's rows, while `firm_id`'s integrity is already protected by `NOT NULL`, immutability, the row policy's `WITH CHECK`, and population only from a verified `FirmContext`. `PA-007` adds no such column and no such constraint; the decision binds every future Firm-scoped relation.

11. **No uniqueness on any business attribute, permanently.** The canonical Firm name carries **no unique constraint, no unique index, and no partial unique index**, now or ever. Uniqueness checks run with row security suspended (§8.5), so a global constraint on a law firm's name would return a violation to a caller who cannot see the conflicting row — an existence disclosure across a conflicts boundary. **The defence is the constraint's absence, not the caller's visibility.** ADR-017 Decision 5's platform-generated-identifier exception covers the primary key alone and never extends to a business attribute; the same reasoning forbids a unique constraint on the jurisdiction reference or any other non-identifier column.

12. **No caching, memoization, singleton, or static state.** A registry answer is read afresh on every call. Introducing a cache would require its own approved decision defining invalidation and version awareness, and `PA-007` contracts none.

### Exact scope and deliverable

**One relation** — the sole ADR-016 Decision 4 class-(b) relation, declared as class (b) in the migration that creates it, carrying the `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §5.2 module-name prefix:

- **`id uuid PRIMARY KEY`** — the Firm's own identity, supplied by the application as a `PF-048` UUIDv7 through `PA-001`'s `FirmId`. Native PostgreSQL `uuid`, never `char(36)`, `varchar`, `text`, or `bytea` (ADR-017 Decision 1). **No `DEFAULT`, no `gen_random_uuid()`, no server-side UUID function, no trigger, and no sequence** (ADR-017 Decision 3).
- **No `firm_id` column**, and no column that could serve as one. A Firm's identity *is* the primary key, not a tenancy column.
- **Canonical name** — `text NOT NULL`, storing exactly the value `FirmName` canonicalizes (valid UTF-8, Unicode-aware trim, no control characters, NFC, 1–200 code points). **No unique constraint of any kind** (decision 11). **No collation, case-folding, or normalization is performed by the database**; the canonical form arrives already normalized from the domain type.
- **Jurisdiction reference** — `text NOT NULL`, storing exactly the value `FirmJurisdiction` canonicalizes. **No foreign key**: it is an opaque identifier-only reference across a context boundary to a Legal Intelligence `Jurisdiction` that does not exist, and no foreign key crosses a context boundary. **The value is never parsed** by the relation, a constraint, an index, or a query.
- **Lifecycle state** — `text NOT NULL` with a `CHECK` constraint admitting exactly the four `FirmLifecycleState` cases (`Requested`, `Provisioned`, `Active`, `Closed`) and nothing else. A `text` column with a check is chosen over a PostgreSQL enum type because ADR-020 Decision 2's expand/contract discipline makes changing an enum type a lock-taking schema operation with no compatible retreat, whereas a check constraint is replaceable in one migration. **`FirmLifecycleState` stays un-backed**: the storage representation is the adapter's mapping, declared in `PA-007`'s Infrastructure layer, and **no backing value is added to the published domain enum**. There is **no suspended, disabled, locked, frozen, terminated, or deleted state**, and none may be added.
- **`created_at timestamptz NOT NULL`** and **`updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL`** — the §7.1 baseline for a mutable relation, with **no database default**: a timestamp originates from the injected `PF-047` `Clock`, never from `now()`, `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`, or a column default (§7.2). `timestamp without time zone` is prohibited.
- **No `deleted_at`**, no soft-deletion column, and no status column duplicating the lifecycle state. **No version, sequence, or concurrency column** (decision 4). **No Firm business data of any kind, and no field from which any could be reconstructed** — no client, matter, task, document, communication, financial, branding, domain, hostname, contact, address, seat-count, entitlement, or usage column.
- **Indexes** — the primary-key index only. No index exists whose only purpose is to serve a cross-Firm query, and no index is created on the canonical name or the jurisdiction reference, because no approved query filters, sorts, or matches on either.

**No Row-Level Security policy, and that absence is the decision.** The relation is consulted *in order to* establish `FirmContext`, so a context-comparing predicate would deny every pre-context read and make tenant resolution impossible. **A permissive `USING (true)` policy is prohibited outright as security theatre** — it would present as a control while permitting everything, and would make a genuinely restricted relation indistinguishable from an unrestricted one in any audit of policies. The migration therefore creates **no policy at all**, and the guard test asserts that none exists.

**Dedicated privileges** — granted to the roles **`PF-074`** creates:

- the **migration role** owns the relation and holds its DDL; it never serves a request;
- the **application runtime role** holds `SELECT` narrowed to the columns the published query returns, holds **no `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `TRUNCATE`, `REFERENCES`, or DDL** on it, and holds **no general enumeration access**;
- the **outbox publication role** holds `SELECT` only, per ADR-019 Decision 9;
- **no role anywhere receives `BYPASSRLS`, a superuser attribute, an owner exemption, or a role-based permissive policy**, and no such grant is introduced to make registry access work.

**One published query contract**, framework-free, in `PlatformAdministration`'s Application layer:

- an interface declaring exactly one method, accepting a `PA-001` `FirmId` and returning a minimized result or `null`;
- a `final readonly` result type carrying exactly the **Firm identifier and the lifecycle state** — **not** the canonical name, **not** the jurisdiction reference, and nothing else, because a read that returns more fields than the caller's purpose needs is a defect rather than an optimization (§2.5);
- **no list, search, filter, sort, count, paginate, aggregate, exists-by-name, or exists-by-domain method**, and no method accepting anything other than an exact identifier.

**One PostgreSQL implementation**, in `PlatformAdministration`'s Infrastructure layer, depending on Laravel's concrete `Illuminate\Database\Connection` and nothing else, using bound parameters exclusively, interpolating no value into SQL, opening no transaction of its own, and calling no HTTP client, queue, cache, event dispatcher, or logger.

**One migration**, in the module's own `Database/` directory per ADR-020 Decision 11, declaring the relation class, forward-only in production, with a `down()` that is development convenience and never the production recovery plan.

### Registry access posture — the three mandatory compensating controls, stated honestly

ADR-016 Decision 4a names three controls and calls all three mandatory. `PA-007` delivers two of them and records precisely what the third depends on:

1. **Dedicated database privileges — delivered by `PA-007`, conditional on `PF-074`.** The `GRANT` statements are `PA-007`'s and target the roles `PF-074` creates. `PF-074` is now `Done` and those roles exist on `main`, so this control is deliverable and this condition of `PA-007`'s Definition of Ready is satisfied. Delivering it remains `PA-007`'s own separately authorized work.
2. **Narrow named query contracts — delivered by `PA-007` in full.** One method, one exact-identifier input, a two-field result, and no enumeration surface anywhere.
3. **Application authorization composed first — not delivered by `PA-007`, because `PA-007` exposes no caller surface.** It creates no route, controller, middleware, container binding, or service provider, so there is no `PlatformAdministration` request path here for authorization to compose in front of. The obligation binds every future caller — `PF-081`, `PF-082`, and any later `PlatformAdministration` path — and each must compose its own authorization first, exactly as any other protected read does (Constitution Article 28). **`PA-007` makes no claim that control 3 is satisfied anywhere.**

### Candidate discovery, confidentiality, and fail-closed behaviour

- **Registry readability is not membership, authentication, authorization, entitlement, or permission.** Learning that a Firm exists grants nothing inside it. `FirmContext` is still constructed only from verified identity and active verified membership, which IdentityAccess alone establishes. **A candidate Firm reference grants no Firm access**, and a Firm identifier is not a secret and not a capability.
- **Three outcomes must be externally indistinguishable**: a lookup that finds nothing, a lookup the caller is not authorized to make, and a lookup that cannot be authoritatively completed. No count, list, ranking, aggregate, search hit, timing signal, error text, or response shape distinguishes them, and no failure discloses whether any Firm exists.
- **Inside the application the query distinguishes exactly two results, so that callers can fail closed**: a minimized result, or `null` for not-found. Any infrastructure failure raises **one** fixed-message exception with **no** identifier, candidate value, reason code, cause discriminator, chained driver exception, or SQLSTATE in its message or properties. **A caller must treat that exception as a denial** — never as "not found", never as a reason to retry into success, and never as a degraded mode. **Availability never outranks Firm isolation or authorization.**
- **No exception message, log line, event, metric, or property contains a Firm identifier, canonical name, jurisdiction reference, candidate value, or rejected input** — the same rule `PA-001` already holds.
- **No cross-Firm list, search, export, report, analytics, benchmarking, comparison, or unrestricted enumeration**, in this context, ever. No `PlatformAdministration` record, query, cache, projection, index, or event spans two Firms.

### Dependency direction

```text
Interface        (none — PA-007 creates no interface layer)
   ↓
Application      PlatformAdministration query contract + minimized result type
                 framework-free; no Laravel, Eloquent, HTTP, queue, or database symbol
   ↓
Domain           PA-001 types, consumed unchanged
   ↑
Infrastructure   PostgreSQL implementation + migration
                 depends on the Application contract and the Domain types;
                 the dependency never points the other way
```

**No framework or database object leaks into the domain contract.** The Domain layer stays exactly as `PA-001` left it — not one file modified. The Application layer stays framework-free and is asserted so. Only the Infrastructure layer may name `Illuminate\Database\Connection`, and it may name nothing else from any vendor namespace.

### Security, privacy, and audit rules

- **No credential, reusable secret, session token, MFA secret, or recovery material** is stored, read, logged, cached, or carried anywhere in `PA-007`.
- **No personal data belonging to a Firm's clients**, and no Firm business content, enters the relation, the query result, an exception, or a test fixture.
- **No Firm identifier is placed in a URL parameter, query string, or any position routinely logged by an intermediary** — `PA-007` creates no transport surface at all.
- **`PA-007` claims no audit persistence and asserts no actor attribution** (decision 8), emits no domain event, writes no outbox row, and dispatches nothing.
- **No existing guard, protected check, test, assertion, static-analysis level, formatting rule, security control, architecture decision, or approval gate is removed, weakened, renamed, skipped, or suppressed.** Where an existing guard must change because `PA-007` legitimately creates a file or directory it asserts absent, the change is a **strengthening**, specified in advance below — never a deletion.

### Guard amendments, specified in advance rather than discovered mid-implementation

`PA-001` collided with five Foundation guards during implementation because its contract had not anticipated them. `PA-007` will collide with more, and names them now so the implementation stops at none of them:

- **`tests/Unit/Modules/PlatformAdministration/Domain/PlatformAdministrationContractShapeTest.php`** holds an exact-equality allowlist over every `.php` file under `app/Modules`, asserts that the module's `Application/`, `Infrastructure/`, and `Database/` directories **do not exist**, and bans the literal substrings `Migration`, `Schema`, `Blueprint`, `Repository`, `Eloquent`, and `ServiceProvider` in module source. Each new `PA-007` file is added to the allowlist; the three directory-absence assertions for directories `PA-007` legitimately creates are **replaced by per-layer content assertions**, never deleted; the remaining four directory-absence assertions (`Interface/`, `Routes/`, `Config/`, in-module `Tests/`) and the `ModuleServiceProvider.php` absence assertion are **retained unchanged**.
- **The framework-namespace and global-helper denylists are split per layer, and the result is stricter, not weaker.** `Domain/` and `Application/` keep the existing denylist unchanged and gain the assertion that they are framework-free by construction. `Infrastructure/` receives an **exact-equality allowlist** of permitted imports — exactly `Illuminate\Database\Connection` plus `PlatformAdministration`'s own symbols — mirroring `FirmTransactionManagerTest::test_the_adapter_imports_and_invokes_no_external_client()`. An exact allowlist is strictly stronger than the denylist it replaces for that layer, and **no assertion is removed without a stronger replacement**.
- **`tests/Unit/Infrastructure/Database/FirmTransactionManagerTest.php::test_no_production_source_outside_the_adapter_writes_the_firm_setting()`** scans all of `app/` for `onelegalpro.firm_id`, `set_config(`, and `SET`/`RESET … firm_id`. **It must pass byte-identical and unchanged**, which decision 9 guarantees by construction. If it does not, that is a defect in the implementation, never a reason to edit the guard.
- **Any guard amendment beyond those named here requires a separately recorded owner authorization before the file is edited**, on the `PA-001` and `PF-040` precedent. The implementation stops and reports rather than improvising.

### Dependencies

*Done and consumed:*

- **`PA-001` — Firm Aggregate Foundation — Done.** `Firm`, `FirmId`, `FirmName`, `FirmJurisdiction`, `FirmLifecycleState`, and `FirmCreated` are consumed **unchanged**; not one `PA-001` file is modified.
- **`PF-048` UUIDv7, `PF-044` BusinessIdentifier, `PF-047` Clock, `PF-049` Foundation Exception Hierarchy, `PF-042` ValueObject — Done.** Consumed unchanged.
- **`PF-033` PostgreSQL Continuous Integration — Done.** The required `Application Tests` check runs on PostgreSQL 16 as a non-superuser `NOBYPASSRLS` role, which is what makes a genuine privilege test possible at all.

*Done and deliberately **not** consumed:*

- **`PF-073` Transaction Manager — Done.** Not imported and not invoked (decision 9).
- **`PF-080` Firm Context — Done.** Not constructed, not imported, not consumed. The registry exists precisely *before* it.

*Approved architecture:* ADR-016 Decisions 4, 4a, 10, 12, 15; ADR-017 Decisions 1, 3, 5, 8, 9, 11; ADR-019 Decision 9; ADR-020 Decisions 1, 2, 5, 6, 8, 11, 13; ADR-021 Decision 12; `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §2.4, §2.5, §4.1, §4.4, §5.1, §5.2, §7.1, §7.2, §7.4, §8.3, §8.5, §9.2, §15.5, §16.3, §22.1; `docs/architecture/19_Platform_Administration_Architecture.md` §6, §7, §14, §16, §17, §18, §19; `docs/architecture/16_Identity_Security_Access_Control_Architecture.md` §29; `docs/architecture/04_Security_Architecture.md`; Constitution Articles 27, 28, 45–48.

*Absent, mandatory, and therefore blocking:*

- **`PF-074` — PostgreSQL Runtime Role Separation — `Done`, merged to `main` on 16 August 2026 by pull request #61 (merge commit `835e04f1`); full contract recorded and independently reviewed under EPIC-001 above. Nothing is deployed.** ADR-016 Decision 10 and `docs/architecture/03_Database_Design.md` §4.1 define three platform-wide logical roles — migration, application runtime, outbox publication — governing **every** relation, and prohibit "a single database account used for migrations and for serving requests". **Before `PF-074` merged, `main` had no such separation**: `config/database.php` held one stock `pgsql` connection with no role split, and the required `Application Tests` job created one role that owned the database, ran the migrations, and served the requests — exactly the prohibited configuration. **`PF-074` replaced that arrangement on `main`**, and the three separated roles are now provisioned by the tracked platform bootstrap migration. **That is platform infrastructure, which ADR-020 Decision 11 assigns to Platform Foundation and which Constitution Article 45 places outside `PlatformAdministration`'s three permitted concepts.** Compensating control 1 is therefore now deliverable. **`PF-074` is `Done`, so it no longer blocks `PA-007`** — which still requires its own separate owner authorization before implementation begins.
- **`PF-064` — Module Migration Registration — `Done`, merged to `main` on 17 August 2026 by pull request #62 (merge commit `bb7f94a3`); contract recorded under EPIC-001 above. Nothing is deployed.** ADR-020 Decision 11 and §5.1 require a module's migrations to live in its own `Database/` directory. **Before `PF-064` merged there was no mechanism by which such a directory was discovered or run**; `PF-064` supplies exactly that mechanism on `main`, through one shared application-level provider and one bootstrap registration. Placing the migration in `database/migrations/` instead would violate §5.1, and "historical migrations are never edited" makes a later relocation a new migration rather than a move. Inventing a registration mechanism inside `PA-007` would pre-create another story's type and would be exactly the invented standard `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`'s own rules forbid.

  **`PA-007` does not assert that `PF-060`, `PF-061`, and `PF-063` are required, and an earlier draft that did so is withdrawn.** Those identifiers exist as **names only**, and no accepted contract apportions migration discovery among them. The analysis recorded under EPIC-001 establishes that the **minimum genuine prerequisite is one narrow capability** — registering each approved module's migration directory with Laravel's migrator — that **`PF-060` and `PF-062` are excluded on evidence**, and that assigning the capability to `PF-061` or `PF-063` would have silently redefined a story with a name but no contract. **The technical owner therefore assigned `PF-064` on 13 August 2026**, leaving `PF-060` through `PF-063` untouched for their own later analysis.

*Not required, because deliberately out of scope:* IdentityAccess EPIC-009 stages 1 and 2 — neither the relation nor its query authenticates anyone or resolves membership, so `PA-007` can be implemented before IdentityAccess exists. `PF-081` and `PF-082` — `PA-007` implements neither and depends on neither. `PA-006` — no audit is claimed (decision 8). `PF-091`/`PF-092` — no outbox row is written (decision 7). `PF-045` Money and `PF-046` Result — no monetary or result type appears anywhere.

*Future dependents, never dependencies:* **`PA-008`** — the `CandidateFirmDirectory` adapter, which consumes this story's published query; `PA-002` through `PA-006`; `PF-081`; `PF-082`; `PF-091`; `PF-092`; every Firm-scoped repository and Row-Level Security policy.

### Definition of Ready — met (17 August 2026); implementation not authorized

*Resolved:* objective; owner; ownership boundary; scope and deliverable; the exact relation, its columns, constraints, indexes, and absent columns; the Row-Level-Security posture and the prohibition on a permissive policy; the privilege model; the query contract and its minimized result; the lookup-input decision; the concurrency decision; the idempotency decision; the foreign-key decision; the uniqueness prohibition; the transaction-boundary decision; fail-closed and confidentiality behaviour; dependency direction; guard amendments; acceptance criteria; test plan; validation plan; allowed and forbidden files; exclusions.

*Resolved 17 August 2026 — `PA-007`'s Definition of Ready is now **met**.* Both mandatory dependencies are `Done` and merged to `main`: **`PF-074`** PostgreSQL Runtime Role Separation (pull request #61, merge commit `835e04f1`) and **`PF-064`** Module Migration Registration (pull request #62, merge commit `bb7f94a3`). These were the two conditions the technical owner fixed on 13 August 2026, and they were independent of each other throughout. `PF-074` supplies compensating control 1 of the three ADR-016 Decision 4a calls mandatory — decisive here because the registry deliberately carries **no** Row-Level Security policy, so privileges are its only database-level control. `PF-064` supplies the discovery and execution path a module-owned migration requires. Neither was substituted by a stub, a single shared account, a temporary grant, a `USING (true)` policy, a root-directory migration, an ad-hoc service provider, or a `loadMigrationsFrom` call bolted onto an unrelated provider.

**Ready is not authorization, and `PA-007` remains `Backlog`.** Meeting the Definition of Ready records that the blocking dependencies are satisfied and nothing more. **This tracking pass authorizes no `PA-007` implementation, deployment, production access, schema creation, migration execution, role creation, grant, or security change.** `PA-007` is a database-design and security/authorization change under `AGENTS.md`, so it additionally requires its own separate, explicit owner authorization before any file is edited. It is **not** In Progress, Code Review, Architecture Review, QA, Approved, or Done.

**Therefore `PA-007` remains `Backlog`.** Accepting this contract makes it neither Ready nor In Progress, schedules nothing, and authorizes nothing.

### Acceptance criteria

A future `PA-007` implementation is correct only if all of the following hold:

1. Exactly one new relation exists, declared **class (b)** in the migration that creates it, carrying the module-name prefix, and it is the **only** relation in that class.
2. The relation has **no `firm_id` column**, and its primary key is `id uuid` carrying the Firm's own identity.
3. The primary key has **no database-generated default, function, trigger, or sequence**; every identifier arrives from the application as a `PF-048` UUIDv7.
4. The relation carries exactly the columns in *Exact scope and deliverable* — identity, canonical name, jurisdiction reference, lifecycle state, `created_at`, `updated_at` — and **no other column**. A structural test enumerates the actual columns and asserts the set exactly, so a later migration cannot quietly add a business-data column.
5. The lifecycle column carries a `CHECK` constraint admitting exactly `Requested`, `Provisioned`, `Active`, `Closed` and nothing else, and `FirmLifecycleState` remains **un-backed** in the domain.
6. Both timestamp columns are `timestamptz`, `NOT NULL`, and carry **no database default**; no `timestamp without time zone` column exists.
7. **No unique constraint or unique index exists on any column other than the primary key** — asserted by enumerating the relation's constraints and indexes, not by inspecting migration source.
8. **No foreign key constraint exists on the relation, in either direction**, and no other relation references it.
9. **No Row-Level Security policy exists on the relation** — asserted against `pg_policies`, not against migration source — and Row-Level Security is neither enabled nor forced on it. **No `USING (true)` policy exists anywhere in the database.**
10. The application runtime role holds `SELECT` on the relation and holds **no `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `TRUNCATE`, `REFERENCES`, or DDL** privilege on it — asserted by querying the catalogue as that role, not by reading the migration.
11. **No role in the database holds `rolsuper` or `rolbypassrls`**, and the request-serving role is not the relation's owner — asserted against `pg_roles` and `pg_class`.
12. The migration role and the request-serving role are **distinct accounts**, asserted by connecting as each and observing different `current_user` values with different privilege sets.
13. The published query contract declares **exactly one** method, accepting exactly a `FirmId` and returning exactly the minimized result type or `null`. It declares **no** list, search, filter, sort, count, paginate, aggregate, by-name, by-domain, or by-hostname method — asserted by enumerating the interface's methods, not by name-probing.
14. The minimized result type is `final readonly`, exposes exactly the Firm identifier and the lifecycle state, and exposes **no** canonical name, jurisdiction reference, timestamp, or any other field.
15. The PostgreSQL implementation uses **bound parameters exclusively**; no identifier, name, or caller-supplied value is interpolated into any SQL string.
16. The implementation opens **no transaction**, imports and invokes **no** HTTP client, queue, cache, event dispatcher, logger, or vendor SDK, and imports exactly `Illuminate\Database\Connection` and `PlatformAdministration`'s own symbols and nothing else.
17. **No production file writes the relation** — no `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `UPSERT`, or `TRUNCATE` statement exists in any `PA-007` production source, asserted by scanning the delivered files.
18. **No `PA-007` file contains the literal `onelegalpro.firm_id` or calls `set_config(`**, and `FirmTransactionManager` is neither imported nor invoked.
19. **No `PA-007` file references `CandidateFirmDirectory`, `CandidateFirmReference`, `CandidateFirmSourceKind`, `App\Application\Tenancy`, `FirmContext`, `FirmMembership`, `FirmProvisioning`, `SubscriptionEntitlement`, `EthicalWall`, or `PrivilegedAccessGrant`.**
20. **No hostname, domain, host-mapping, `TenantDomain`, branding, email-domain, or slug column, index, parameter, or method exists anywhere.**
21. `Domain/` and `Application/` files reference **no** framework namespace and call **no** Laravel global helper; every `PA-007` file declares `strict_types=1`; every namespace agrees with its path.
22. No `PA-001` Domain file is modified; `app/Foundation/**` and `app/Infrastructure/**` production source are unmodified; `tests/Unit/Infrastructure/Database/FirmTransactionManagerTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Infrastructure/Database/FirmTransactionManagerPostgreSqlTest.php`, `tests/Unit/Foundation/FoundationLayerHasNoFrameworkDependenciesTest.php`, and `tests/Feature/PostgreSqlTestDatabaseGuardTest.php` all pass **byte-identical and unchanged**.
23. No version, sequence, or concurrency column, member, or parameter exists; no idempotency key, record, or relation exists; no audit relation, column, or record exists; no outbox row, domain event, or dispatch exists; no cache, memo, static, or singleton state exists.
24. No route, controller, middleware, container binding, service provider, seeder, factory, or console command is created; no `Interface/`, `Routes/`, `Config/`, or in-module `Tests/` directory exists; and `PA-007` itself creates no `ModuleServiceProvider.php`.
25. Every guard amendment is one of those named in *Guard amendments*, each is a strengthening with no assertion removed without a stronger replacement, and each is mutation-tested to prove it is not vacuous.
26. `composer.json`, `composer.lock`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `phpunit.xml`, `phpstan.neon.dist`, `pint.json`, `Dockerfile`, `compose.yaml`, and environment files are unchanged, and no dependency is added.
27. The four required `Protect main` check names are unchanged.

### Focused test plan

*Pure unit suites*, extending `PHPUnit\Framework\TestCase` directly, booting no Laravel application and touching no database, with every test double declared **inside the test file** per the standing rule that no test double belongs under `app/`:

- a **contract-shape suite** proving by reflection the exact interface method set and signature, the minimized result type's exact shape, the layer-by-layer import rules, the absent method names enumerated rather than probed, and the absence of every forbidden symbol in criteria 19 and 20;
- an **implementation suite** driving the PostgreSQL implementation against a recording `Connection` double, proving that every statement binds its parameters, that no identifier is interpolated, that no transaction is opened, that not-found returns `null`, that an infrastructure failure raises one fixed-message exception with no chained cause and no identifier in the message, and that two calls perform two reads so nothing is cached.

*Gated PostgreSQL feature suites*, using the existing `REQUIRE_POSTGRESQL_TEST_DATABASE` pattern (`addToAssertionCount(1)` and return when unarmed, never `markTestSkipped`), so ordinary local SQLite runs stay supported while the required check runs them armed:

- a **schema/structure suite** asserting against the catalogue — not migration source — the exact column set and types, the absence of `firm_id`, the absence of any default on the primary key and both timestamps, the lifecycle `CHECK` contents, the absence of every unique constraint but the primary key, the absence of every foreign key, and the absence of any Row-Level Security policy on the relation;
- a **privilege suite** connecting as the request-serving role and asserting `SELECT` present and `INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE`/`TRUNCATE`/`REFERENCES`/DDL absent; asserting no role holds `rolsuper` or `rolbypassrls`; asserting the request-serving role is not the relation's owner; and asserting the migration role and the request-serving role are distinct `current_user` values;
- a **behavioural query suite** seeding rows as test-local fixtures and proving an exact-identifier hit returns exactly the two minimized fields, a miss returns `null`, and a second Firm's row is never returned for the first Firm's identifier.

*Required negative controls and mutation proofs* — each guard must be proved non-vacuous, on the `PA-001` precedent:

- a synthetic extra column added to a scratch copy of the schema is **detected** by the column-set assertion;
- a synthetic `USING (true)` policy created on a scratch relation is **detected** by the policy assertion;
- a synthetic unique index on the name column is **detected** by the uniqueness assertion;
- a synthetic `INSERT` privilege granted to the request-serving role on a scratch relation is **detected** by the privilege assertion;
- a synthetic reference to a forbidden symbol injected into a copy of each delivered production file is **detected** by the shape assertions;
- a synthetic interpolated identifier injected into a copy of the implementation is **detected** by the parameter-binding assertion.

A guard that cannot fail is not a guard, and each of the above is recorded with its observed detection.

### Full validation plan

In the canonical Docker PHP 8.4 / PostgreSQL 16 environment, against a disposable non-superuser `NOBYPASSRLS` role and database per the documented `CONTRIBUTING.md` recipe, with the engine guard **armed** and **no SQLite substitution**: `git diff --check`; `composer validate --strict`; `composer pint:test`; `composer phpstan` at level 5 with no baseline, suppression, or ignored error; every focused suite above; the `PF-049` Foundation guard, the `PF-033` PostgreSQL engine/role guard, both `FirmTransactionManager` suites, and the `PA-001` `FirmTest` and `FirmValueObjectsTest` suites passing **byte-identical and unchanged**; the complete PostgreSQL-backed suite passing with a recorded test and assertion count strictly above the measured pre-implementation baseline; and `composer audit --locked --abandoned=report` and `npm audit --audit-level=high` both clean. Independent review and architecture review record no unresolved P0, P1, or P2.

**The four required `Protect main` checks remain exactly:** `PHP Code Quality`, `Frontend Build`, `Application Tests`, `Dependency Audit`.

### Allowed implementation files

A future `PA-007` implementation — once this contract is accepted, **and** both blocking dependencies exist, **and** the database-design and security/authorization approval gates in `AGENTS.md` are explicitly authorized — may create or change **only**:

- `app/Modules/PlatformAdministration/Application/Queries/FindFirmRegistryEntry.php`;
- `app/Modules/PlatformAdministration/Application/Queries/FirmRegistryEntry.php`;
- `app/Modules/PlatformAdministration/Application/Queries/FirmRegistryUnavailable.php`;
- `app/Modules/PlatformAdministration/Infrastructure/Persistence/PostgreSqlFirmRegistry.php`;
- one migration under `app/Modules/PlatformAdministration/Database/Migrations/`, creating the relation and its grants;
- `tests/Unit/Modules/PlatformAdministration/Application/FirmRegistryQueryContractShapeTest.php`;
- `tests/Unit/Modules/PlatformAdministration/Infrastructure/PostgreSqlFirmRegistryTest.php`;
- `tests/Feature/Modules/PlatformAdministration/FirmRegistrySchemaTest.php`;
- `tests/Feature/Modules/PlatformAdministration/FirmRegistryPrivilegeTest.php`;
- `tests/Feature/Modules/PlatformAdministration/FirmRegistryQueryPostgreSqlTest.php`;
- `tests/Unit/Modules/PlatformAdministration/Domain/PlatformAdministrationContractShapeTest.php`, **only** for the guard amendments named above;
- `app/Modules/PlatformAdministration/README.md`;
- `docs/implementation/03_Engineering_Backlog.md` and `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`, for verified lifecycle and evidence updates;
- `docs/implementation/01_Implementation_Sprint_Plan.md`, for the lifecycle note only.

Any additional source, test, provider, configuration, workflow, migration, or database file requires a separately reviewed contract correction and explicit approval before editing.

### Forbidden files and changes

No change to any `PA-001` Domain file; to `app/Foundation/**`; to `app/Infrastructure/Database/FirmTransactionManager.php` or its two suites; to `tests/Unit/Foundation/FoundationLayerHasNoFrameworkDependenciesTest.php` or `tests/Feature/PostgreSqlTestDatabaseGuardTest.php`; to `config/database.php`, `compose.yaml`, `Dockerfile`, `docker/**`, `.env`, `.env.example`, `.github/**`, `.githooks/**`, `composer.json`, `composer.lock`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `phpunit.xml`, `phpstan.neon.dist`, or `pint.json`; to `database/migrations/**`, `database/seeders/**`, or `database/factories/**`; to Laravel bootstrap, configuration, providers, routes, controllers, or middleware; or to `app/Application/**` and `app/Http/**`. **No architecture or ADR file may be edited by the implementation story.** **No required check, test, assertion, guard, static-analysis level, formatting rule, security control, or approval gate may be removed, weakened, renamed, skipped, or suppressed.**

### Explicit exclusions

`PA-007` delivers none of, and must not stub, approximate, simulate, partially implement, or rename any of: the concrete `CandidateFirmDirectory` adapter or any `App\Application\Tenancy` file; any enumeration, list, search, count, aggregate, ranking, export, report, analytics, benchmarking, or comparison capability; any hostname, custom-domain, `TenantDomain`, branding, domain-ownership, email-domain, or slug mapping; any registry write, insert, update, delete, or lifecycle transition; `FirmProvisioning`; `SubscriptionEntitlement`; entitlement or seat-limit evaluation; administrative audit persistence, actor attribution, or security-event recording; outbox rows, publication, dispatch, or per-Firm iteration; idempotency records or keys; optimistic-concurrency version columns; caching, memoization, or singleton state; authentication, credentials, MFA, sessions, principals, membership, invitations, recovery, delegation, or privileged-access grants; authorization decisions, roles, capabilities, or permissions; `FirmContext` construction or `PF-081` resolution; `PF-082` middleware, HTTP extraction, request adaptation, or container binding; **Ethical Walls of any shape**; PostgreSQL role creation, `config/database.php` connections, Docker or workflow changes; module loading, registration, or service-provider mechanics; and every other business module. **`PA-002` through `PA-006`, `PA-008`, `PF-074`, `PF-081`, and `PF-082` are not silently implemented, in whole or in part, by `PA-007`.**

### Documentation impact

This entry; the `PA-007` lifecycle note in `docs/implementation/01_Implementation_Sprint_Plan.md`; the `PA-007` status and sequence records in `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`; and the remaining-stages table and named sequence in `app/Modules/PlatformAdministration/README.md`, whose standing boundary record genuinely changes because the adapter and the aggregate version both leave `PA-007`'s scope. **No architecture or ADR file is edited, and none needs to be.**

Two issues were found in preparing this contract, and both are now closed without amending an approved document. The **`PF-081` B4 ordering question** (decision 1) is resolved by the technical owner's 13 August 2026 decision separating implementation-start blockers from the operational-completeness blocker; that is a tracking correction to how B4's gate is recorded, and it alters no `PF-081` decision, API, acceptance criterion, allowed file, exclusion, or status. The **absence of any relation in which a pre-`FirmContext` registry read could be audited** (decision 8) is **not a contradiction**: §2.5 requires auditing "where required", nothing currently requires it for this read, and `PA-007` resolves it within the story contract by claiming no audit persistence at all and recording the gap for `PA-006`. **No remaining contradiction requires an architecture or ADR amendment.**

### Definition of Done

`PA-007` is Done only when: this contract has passed independent review and received recorded final technical acceptance; both blocking dependencies exist and are Done; the `AGENTS.md` database-design and security/authorization approval gates are explicitly authorized and recorded; the accepted contract is implemented strictly within its allowlist; every acceptance criterion above passes; every focused suite passes with the engine guard armed against real PostgreSQL; every required negative control is observed to detect its synthetic defect; the unchanged Foundation, PostgreSQL-guard, `FirmTransactionManager`, and `PA-001` suites pass byte-identical; the complete canonical PostgreSQL-backed suite passes with no removed, weakened, renamed, skipped, or suppressed test, assertion, guard, static-analysis level, formatting rule, security control, or approval gate; Pint, PHPStan level 5, `composer validate --strict`, both dependency audits, and `git diff --check` pass; independent review reports no unresolved P0, P1, or P2; **all four required protected checks pass on the exact final implementation head**; the required human approval comment is recorded; the PR merges to `main`; tracking records only verified evidence; and the implementation branch and worktree are removed locally and remotely.

**Done would make no claim** that `PA-008`, the `CandidateFirmDirectory` adapter, `PF-081` tenant resolution, `PF-082` middleware, tenant isolation as a whole, Row-Level Security on any Firm-scoped relation, Firm-scoped repositories, `FirmProvisioning`, `SubscriptionEntitlement`, entitlement or seat-limit enforcement, administrative audit persistence, actor attribution, IdentityAccess, or any other business module exists — or that `PF-081` blocker B4 is cleared, that an executable candidate-resolution path exists, that Firm isolation is proven end to end, or that Release 0.1 is production-ready.

### Sequencing decision and milestone effect

**Approved sequence, recorded by the technical owner on 13 August 2026:**

**`PA-007`** registry and narrow query → **IdentityAccess EPIC-009 stages 1 and 2** → **`PF-081`** port and resolver → **`PA-008`** adapter → **`PF-082`** middleware and composition.

`PA-007` is itself preceded by **`PF-074`** PostgreSQL Runtime Role Separation and **`PF-064`** Module Migration Registration, both recorded under EPIC-001. Both had to be `Done` before `PA-007`'s Definition of Ready could be met; **both are now `Done` and merged, so that condition is satisfied.** The two were independent of each other throughout. **`PA-001` is `Done` and precedes all of it.** `PA-007` nevertheless remains `Backlog` and unauthorized.

**`PF-081` now precedes the adapter, and that is the corrected reading of B4 rather than a reordering `PA-007` performs.** The adapter cannot precede the port it implements. Per the same decision, **B1, B2, and B3 gate `PF-081`'s implementation start; B4 gates its operational completeness and integration.** `PF-081`'s decisions, API sketch, acceptance criteria, allowed files, exclusions, test plan, security rules, ownership boundaries, and status are unaltered, and `PF-081` remains `Backlog`.

**Effect on `PF-081`'s blockers.** **B1 — partially cleared, and only on completion.** `PA-001` cleared the aggregate and owned-`FirmId` half; a completed `PA-007` clears the registry-relation half. **B2 and B3 remain fully open** — IdentityAccess EPIC-009 stages 1 and 2 are `Backlog`, and B3 in particular is decisive, because without an authoritative membership result `PF-081` has no authority to construct a `FirmContext` from. **B4 remains open, is not addressable by `PA-007`, and is `PA-008`'s** — it does not block `PF-081` from becoming Ready, and it does block any claim that `PF-081` is operationally complete or integrated. **`PF-081` therefore remains `Backlog` with its Definition of Ready unmet after `PA-007` completes**, because B2 and B3 are still open.

**Assigning `PF-074` and `PA-008` scheduled and authorized nothing.** PF-074 later received its full contract, independent contract review, and separate implementation authorization and is now `Code Review`; PA-008 remains `Backlog` with a scope outline only.

**Milestone effect: none.** **31 August 2026** (public website and sales readiness) and **15 September 2026** (planned secure production service start) remain **targets, not contractual commitments** (`docs/architecture/02_Product_Requirements.md` §1 and §8). Recording a contract advances neither, and the outstanding production-access evidence is unchanged: an executed and recorded restore test, operational monitoring, a documented incident procedure, the Thai-qualified legal review required by `docs/adr/ADR-012-…` Decision 8, and every applicable `AGENTS.md` approval gate.

## PA-008 — CandidateFirmDirectory Adapter — Backlog (scope outline; no full contract)

**Status.** `Backlog`. **Not** Ready, In Progress, Code Review, Architecture Review, QA, Approved, or Done. **No `PA-008` implementation has started and no `PA-008` file exists.** This is a **scope outline recorded on the technical owner's assignment of 13 August 2026**, not a full story contract: it fixes ownership, scope, dependencies, and exclusions so that `PF-081` blocker B4 has a named owner. **`PA-008` still requires its own full contract and its own Definition of Ready before implementation begins**, and **assigning the identifier schedules and authorizes nothing.**

**Objective.** Implement `PF-081`'s `CandidateFirmDirectory` port as a **thin adapter over `PA-007`'s narrow registry query**, so that candidate-Firm discovery has an authoritative implementer and an executable end-to-end candidate-resolution path exists.

**Owner.** `PlatformAdministration`. The port stays `PF-081`'s; the adapter and the registry query it delegates to are this context's. Like `PA-007`, `PA-008` is deliberately **not** one of the six approved ARCH-019 §28 stages. It adds **no fourth concept** to `PlatformAdministration`, which continues to own exactly `Firm`, `FirmProvisioning`, and `SubscriptionEntitlement` (Constitution Article 45) — an adapter over an already-owned query is not a new concept.

**Scope.** One adapter class implementing `App\Application\Tenancy\CandidateFirmDirectory`, translating a `CandidateFirmReference` into a call on `PA-007`'s published query and returning a candidate identifier or `null`. It performs the input normalization and bounds checking that reference requires before any lookup, and nothing else.

**Dependencies — both blocking, and neither exists yet.** **`PA-007`** must be `Done`, because the adapter has nothing to delegate to otherwise; `PA-007` is `Backlog` with its Definition of Ready met but its implementation unauthorized. **`PF-081`** must have published the port, because the adapter has nothing to implement otherwise. That ordering is exactly why B4 gates operational completeness rather than `PF-081`'s implementation start.

**Exclusions.** `PA-008` performs and delivers none of: authentication; membership verification; `FirmContext` construction; authorization decisions of any kind, and **no widening of any authorization outcome**; middleware, HTTP extraction, routing, request adaptation, or container binding; **any change to the `PA-007` registry schema, migration, privileges, constraints, or indexes**; **any enumeration, list, search, count, or aggregate**; caching, memoization, or singleton state; any change to IdentityAccess behaviour; any new candidate key, host or domain mapping, or `TenantDomain` concept; and any change to `PF-081`'s port, resolver, or acceptance criteria.

**Standing rules it inherits unchanged.** A candidate value identifies a **candidate and nothing more** — never an identifier, never proof. **Registry readability is not membership**, and a returned candidate identifier grants no access to any Firm. Not-found, unauthorized, and undeterminable outcomes stay **externally indistinguishable**, and the adapter introduces no message, code, response shape, or timing signal that separates them. **Fail-closed**: an unavailable registry is a denial, never a not-found and never a retry-into-success.

**Guard obligation, declared in advance.** `PF-081`'s `TenancyContractShapeTest` asserts that **no production implementation of `CandidateFirmDirectory` exists anywhere under `app/`** — correct at `PF-081` implementation time, and something `PA-008` necessarily makes false. **`PA-008`'s contract must name that guard amendment up front**, and the replacement must be a **strengthening**: the blanket absence assertion becomes an exact-equality assertion that the only implementer is `PA-008`'s named class at its named path. **No assertion may be removed without a stronger replacement**, on the `PA-001` and `PF-040` precedent. The same obligation applies to `PlatformAdministrationContractShapeTest`, whose file allowlist and forbidden-symbol list both currently exclude the adapter.

**Approval gates.** `PA-008` is an **authorization-adjacent security change** under `AGENTS.md` — it supplies the implementer for a pre-`FirmContext` discovery path — and its own contract must enumerate its allowed files, acceptance criteria, and negative-control proofs before any implementation begins.

## Standing constraints for this epic

- **`PA-001` is `Done`; every other story in this epic is `Backlog`.** None of the others is In Progress, Code Review, Architecture Review, QA, Approved, or Done. `PA-007` is the only one whose Definition of Ready is met. **`PA-002` through `PA-006` carry reserved identifiers with no contract, no Definition of Ready, and no authorization.** **`PA-007` has a recorded contract and its Definition of Ready is now *met*** — its two blocking dependencies, **`PF-074`** and **`PF-064`**, are both `Done` and merged. **It remains `Backlog` and unauthorized**: Ready is not authorization, and as a database-design and security/authorization change it requires its own separate explicit owner approval. **`PA-008` carries a recorded scope outline, not a full contract**, and is blocked on `PA-007` and `PF-081`. **Recording a contract or a scope outline, and assigning an identifier, schedules and authorizes nothing.** Nothing is deployed.
- **Pull requests remain serialized — one active implementation PR at a time**, one approved story per PR, per `CONTRIBUTING.md`.
- **Authentication, authorization, database-design, security-control, and destructive-operation changes each require the explicit human approval gate** in `AGENTS.md` before merge, regardless of this epic's planning status.
- **No control may be weakened to meet a date.** Firm isolation, authorize-before-retrieval, denied-existence confidentiality, actor attribution, human approval, immutable audit, fail-closed behaviour, white-label presentation, and the Thai-language legal-authority rules are not schedule variables.
- **No absent control may be stubbed, approximated, simulated, partially implemented, or renamed**, and the absence of a normally assumed capability is disclosed in-product and in the pilot agreement.
- **`PF-045` — Money and `PF-046` — Result remain `Backlog` and deferred from Release 0.1.** Any proposal to record a monetary value in `SubscriptionEntitlement` reopens `PF-045` as a hard prerequisite and requires a new approved ADR.
- **No production-readiness, certification, or compliance claim is made.** **15 September 2026 is a target, not a contractual commitment**, and production access requires all evidence listed in `docs/architecture/02_Product_Requirements.md` §8. The database-design and deployment-architecture evidence items are approved; the executed restore test, operational monitoring, documented incident procedure, separate Thai-qualified legal review required by ADR-012 Decision 8, and every applicable `AGENTS.md` implementation approval remain outstanding.
